      // Three heroes that FLIP — one frame at a time, no scroll and no
      // in-between state. A wheel gesture flips; so do the two text links
      // bottom-right, which is why there is no dot rail. Background position is
      // orientation-aware, per LabelHeroLanding.tsx: landscape → upper third
      // (that is where faces sit in a press shot), portrait/square → centre.
      // ── THE HERO CAN BE ADVANCED BY TOUCH (2026-09-05, Tom, on his phone) ──
      // ⚠ AT PHONE WIDTH THERE WERE ZERO WORKING AFFORDANCES, so two of the
      // three heroes could not be reached by any gesture:
      //   · onWheel is the only advance, and a touch device never emits wheel;
      //   · the pager dots are pointerEvents: 'none' — an indicator;
      //   · heroOthers, the desktop fallback, is display: none at narrow.
      // Desktop was always correct, which is why it survived: the defect is
      // entirely width-dependent.
      //
      // Worth recording that the ATH deck one surface over already had a thumb
      // gesture. Two surfaces, same interaction, one phone-complete — the same
      // "two resolutions of one question" shape as the hover-audio default.
      //
      // heroGo(i) is now the one advance path. flipWheel keeps the clamp and
      // the cooldown and delegates; the dots call it directly, so a tap and a
      // wheel cannot diverge, and the host's swipe→wheel patch keeps working
      // until it is retired.
      // A flick in either axis — people swipe a full-bleed hero sideways as
      // readily as up. 40px is past a tap's slop and short of a scroll.
      heroTouchStart: (e) => {
        const t = e && e.touches && e.touches[0];
        this._heroTouch = t ? { x: t.clientX, y: t.clientY } : null;
      },
      heroTouchEnd: (e) => {
        const a = this._heroTouch;
        const t = e && e.changedTouches && e.changedTouches[0];
        this._heroTouch = null;
        if (!a || !t) return;
        const dx = t.clientX - a.x, dy = t.clientY - a.y;
        const d = Math.abs(dy) >= Math.abs(dx) ? dy : dx;
        if (Math.abs(d) < 40) return;
        // Up or left moves forward, which is how a carousel reads.
        this.heroGo((st.hero || 0) + (d < 0 ? 1 : -1));
      },
      flipWheel: (e) => {
        const d = e.deltaY;
        if (Math.abs(d) < 6) return;
        this.heroGo((st.hero || 0) + (d > 0 ? 1 : -1));
      },
      heroPhoto: {
        position: 'absolute', top: 0, left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0,
        background: hero.src
          // ── A PER-PHOTO FOCUS, WITH THE RATIO RULE AS THE FALLBACK
          // (2026-09-10, engineering) ──────────────────────────────────────
          // The orientation rule guesses where a subject is from the shape of
          // its frame, which is right often enough to be worth keeping and
          // wrong where it matters: Cuddle Magic is a square photo with six
          // people along the bottom edge (faces 69–80% down), so a 16% crop
          // showed a tree. hero.focus is the measured answer for that row and
          // this defers to it; absent, the behaviour is exactly as before.
          //

          ? 'url(' + this.res(hero.src) + ') center ' + (hero.focus || (hero.ar >= 1 ? '16%' : 'center')) + '/cover no-repeat, #0c0d0f'

      // DEFAULT_HEROES rows 2 and 3 read 'Release title pending'. HEROES is

      // ══ THE HERO'S SPARE CONTROL IS THE HOVER LESSON (2026-09-10, Tom) ═══
      // "The three-dot menu isn't really useful, especially because it says

            br: Object.assign({}, sq, { top: (T + SH - A) + 'px', left: (L + S - A) + 'px', borderBottomWidth: '1px', borderRightWidth: '1px' })
          };

      // ── THE ARMS SIT ON THE GUTTER, WHICH IS THE ONE MARGIN ──────────────

      // The dead shell and the dead handler are DELETED rather than left with a

      // ── ⤢ OPEN IS BACK, AND THE REASONING ABOVE WAS WRONG (2026-09-05) ────
      // ⚠ I DELETED THIS CONTROL ON 2026-09-01 on the argument recorded above:
      // that Grid|Map already moves you between the two views, so a third

      // Grid|Map switches how you look at ONE RECORD. ⤢ Open changes the SET —
      // it takes the constellation the drawer has just named and makes it the

      // A HARDENED card: every box below has a fixed height, so moving from star
      // to star changes the picture and the words and nothing else. Nothing can

      // neighbour names wrapping to two lines used to shove the button off.
      // ══ THE PREVIEW CARD, REBUILT (2026-09-11, Tom) ═════════════════════════
      // "I like card C for hover mode. But I need an actual play button outside
      // the cover, like card B for Player mode… seems like there's room for the
      // cover to be bigger in player mode… the size of the cover could be
      // dynamic, depending on the size of the screen."
      //
      // Four changes, and they interlock:
      //
      // 1 · THE COVER, NOT THE ARTIST PHOTO. The note this replaces argued for a
      //     landscape artist frame because "the transport is already showing the
      //     cover two inches below". That was wrong twice: the transport only

      //     artwork at all; and CLAUDE.md says covers are always square. A card

      // 2 · NOTHING SITS ON THE ARTWORK. No play glyph, no scrim, no badge — the

      //     sets this row's height, so a 44px button in the text column costs

      // 4 · HEIGHT IS NO LONGER FIXED. The old 152px was defended as "hardened",
      //     but hardening was never the point — not reflowing a NEIGHBOUR was.
      //     Every box below still has a fixed height or a line clamp, so moving
      //     star to star still changes only the picture and the words. What the

      starCardStyle: { position: 'relative', padding: '4px 16px 14px', boxSizing: 'border-box', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column' },
      // ⚠ NOT absolute any more. View was pinned to the top-right corner and out

      starCardViewStyle: {

      isNews: view === 'news',

      isNewsPage: route === 'news',

      // ── WHAT NEWS SHOWS WHEN THERE IS NO NEWS (2026-09-04, C2) ────────────

      newsCols: (() => {

                : { display: 'none' }),

              // ── THE ACTION CHIP ACTS (2026-09-09, code audit 2c) ────────────────
        // It was pointer-styled with no handler on all three kinds. Play plays
        // the row's record where it resolves one; Read and Preorder open the
        // row's link. Where neither resolves the chip is absent, not inert —
        // actionStyle's own gate already had the shape for it.
        actionClick: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          if (n[5] === 'play') {

          }
          const href = n[7] || '';
          if (href && typeof window !== 'undefined') window.open(href, '_blank', 'noopener');
        },
        // ── NEWS[7] IS THE ROW'S DESTINATION (declared 2026-09-09) ──────────
        // The chip needs somewhere to go and the tuple stopped at [6], so this
        // names the field rather than reading one that does not exist:
        //
        //   NEWS[7]  a RECORD KEY where the action is 'play' (resolved through

        //
        // Absent, not inert, where it is empty: no destination, no chip. Until

                  ? { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer' }
                  : { padding: '7px 9px', borderRadius: '4px', border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer',

                : { display: 'none' }

      // ── About · 7o / 7s ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

      isAboutPage: route === 'about',
      // Founded-in line, taken from the same description so the two cannot
      // disagree ("Founded in Brooklyn in 2010, …" → "Brooklyn · since 2010").
      // The old hand list had Egghunt at 1993 and Whited Sepulchre in Fort

      aboutKicker: (() => {

      })(),

      aboutGroupStyle: {

      aboutGroup: (() => {

      aboutWallStyle: { minWidth: 0 },

      aboutWall: (() => {
        // Most records with the label first — INDEX carries each artist's count
        // at [3] — then anyone with a photograph to show.
        const list = this.data('INDEX').slice()
          .sort((x, y) => (y[3] || 0) - (x[3] || 0) || (y[1] ? 1 : 0) - (x[1] ? 1 : 0))
          .filter(x => x[1])
          // More rows on a taller screen, not more columns.
          .slice(0, W >= 1900 ? 12 : W >= 1660 ? 8 : 5);
        // ── A RATIO THE BOX CAN SURVIVE (2026-09-11, Tom: "one of the artist
        // photos is just a tiny cropped vertical or horizontal line… make sure
        // they're all full size, non-cropped") ──────────────────────────────
        // INDEX[i][2] went straight into aspectRatio as String(x[2]), so the
        // tile's GEOMETRY was an unvalidated data value. Absent or unparseable
        // gives the literal string "undefined", which CSS drops — and a box
        // with no aspect-ratio and no height collapses onto its caption. That
        // is the sliver: not a crop, a tile with no height, showing whatever
        // 30px of photograph the caption happens to stand in front of.
        //
        // Same family as ar: 1.5 and the hero's hard-coded ar: 1 — a raw
        // number used as layout with nothing between it and the page. CLAMPED
        // rather than only defaulted, because 0.08 and 12 are as destructive as
        // undefined and are what a bad EXIF read actually produces. 0.5–2.0 is
        // 1:2 through 2:1, which holds every real portrait including
        // dave-harrington.jpg at 928×1400 (0.663). Outside it the photograph is
        // still real, so the tile squares up and the image fills by cover
        // rather than the row breaking.
        return list.map((x, i) => ({
          key: 'aw' + i,
          name: x[0],
          count: (x[3] === 1 ? '1 record' : x[3] + ' records'),
          tile: {
            // Each tile keeps its photograph's REAL ratio, so the column still
            // reads as a mosaic rather than a stack of identical boxes.
            position: 'relative', width: '100%', aspectRatio: String(this.arOf(x[2])),
            // ⚠ A FLOOR AS WELL AS A RATIO. aspect-ratio is advisory: it yields
            // the moment anything else determines height, so the caption alone
            // could still win. 120px is below the smallest real tile and above
            // any caption, so it never fights a valid ratio and always catches
            // a collapse.
            minHeight: '120px',
            marginBottom: '10px', breakInside: 'avoid', WebkitColumnBreakInside: 'avoid',
            borderRadius: '7px', overflow: 'hidden', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
            background: 'url(' + this.res(x[1]) + ') center ' + (x[5] || 'center') + '/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)',
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-end'
          },
          // The ARTIST INDEX's caption, verbatim: a lighter scrim, the name at
          // NORMAL weight and partial strength, and no record count. The photo is
          // the content; you read in only if the picture stops you.
          scrim: {
            position: 'absolute', left: 0, right: 0, bottom: 0, height: '40%',
            background: 'linear-gradient(rgba(0,0,0,0), rgba(0,0,0,0.34))', pointerEvents: 'none'
          },
          caption: { position: 'relative', display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', padding: '10px 12px', minWidth: 0 },
          nameStyle: {
            fontSize: '12px', fontWeight: 400, letterSpacing: '0.01em', lineHeight: 1.3,
            color: 'rgba(243,241,234,0.78)', textShadow: '0 1px 6px rgba(0,0,0,0.5)',
            transition: 'color 160ms ease',
            overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'normal'
          },
          countStyle: { display: 'none' }
        }));
      })(),
      aboutContacts: this.data('ABOUT_CONTACTS')
        .map((c, i) => ({ key: 'ac' + i, label: c[0], addr: c[1] })),
      // FOLLOW LIVES HERE NOW (2026-09-03, Tom). The header's Follow control is

      // ── THE PORTAL HEADER (DQ-57) ─────────────────────────────────────────

        width: '30px', height: '30px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '50%',

      // THE LAST ROW CLEARS THE PLAYER (2026-09-01). The bar floats over the

      catPadStyle: {
        // ⚠ WAS 96 ON NARROW, AND 22px SHORT. Its comment derived that as

      padding: narrow
          ? ('22px 14px ' + (barShown ? (barBand + 30) : 30) + 'px')
          : ('0 24px ' + (barShown ? (barBand + 40) : 40) + 'px'),
        boxSizing: 'border-box'
      },
      // ── HOVER AUDIO ON EVERY GRID · RETIRED 2026-09-07 ───────────────────

      // The standardized under-header space, not a flat number: the stepped

      catHeadStyle: { paddingTop: narrow ? 0 : '56px' },
      catTitleStyle: { fontSize: narrow ? '26px' : '34px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.025em', lineHeight: 1.04, color: 'var(--ink)' },
      // --ink55, not --ink40 (2026-09-11): the count line is the page's only

      // CLAIMED --ink55 WAS 7.9:1 AND IT WAS NOT — I read 0.55 as a lightness
      // when it is an ALPHA, and rgba(23,25,27,0.55) composited on white is
      // rgb(127,128,129) at 3.92:1, still under CLAUDE.md's 4.5. The number is
      // recorded here because a wrong figure in a comment licenses the same

      // ── THE REDIRECT SAYS SO (2026-09-14, Tom: "redirect with a note") ────

      phoneMapNoteText: (route === 'ath-all-releases')

      // ⚠ THE FLAG IS NOT THE CONDITION (2026-09-14). This read the flag

      //
      // Gated on phoneNoMap() as well, exactly as menuModesStyle and
      // menuViewRowStyle already are — those three now agree at every width,

      //
      // THE FLAG ITSELF IS LEFT SET, deliberately. Rotating back to portrait
      // re-shows the note rather than silently losing the only explanation of
      // why the link did not land where it pointed. State remembers what
      // happened; style decides whether it is currently true.
      phoneMapNoteStyle: (!st.phoneMapNote || !this.phoneNoMap()) ? { display: 'none' } : {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
        marginTop: '10px', padding: '10px 12px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        borderRadius: '6px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', background: 'var(--tile)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9.5px', lineHeight: 1.5,
        letterSpacing: '0.04em', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer'
      },
      phoneMapNoteXStyle: { marginLeft: 'auto', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '11px', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
      phoneMapNoteDismiss: () => this.setState({ phoneMapNote: 0 }),
      catInfoStyle: { marginTop: '6px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
      // DQ-61 · the trails. Each surface passes only what it knows; crumbTrail
      // owns the shape so three pages cannot disagree about it.
      crumbRowStyle: this.crumbRowStyle(),
      // recCrumbs DELETED 2026-09-06 — the album page's crumb row went with the
      // duplicated header (the cover's block states the same hierarchy). The
      // artist and category pages keep theirs.
      artistCrumbs: this.crumbTrail('artist', { artist: this.curArtist() }),
      // On a category view the middle crumbs are the category index and the
      // value — the pair the build just gave addresses to.
      // ⚠ ONE GATE, not two. I first keyed these on `picks` while the header
      // and its chip key on `setOpen` — and a set can be open with picks empty
      // (a committed browseSel, or a constellation), so the header named a set
      // the crumbs did not. Same "two resolutions of one question" that made
      // the r22 constellation grid nameless. setOpen is the condition; picks
      // only decide WHICH trail.
      catCrumbs: (() => {
        if (!setOpen) return [];
        if (st.conAnchor) {
          // A constellation lives on the map domain, hanging off the gateway —
          // so its trail is not the label's. Two crumbs: the map, then this
          // constellation.
          const gate = this.mapUrl(labelKey);
          return [
            { name: 'Star map', href: gate, last: false },
            { name: 'Constellation · ' + st.conAnchor, href: gate + '/constellation/' + this.slug(st.conAnchor), last: true }
          ].map((c, i, a) => ({
            key: 'cc' + i, name: c.name, href: c.href,
            style: c.last
              ? { color: 'var(--ink)', textDecoration: 'none', pointerEvents: 'none', cursor: 'default' }
              : { color: 'var(--ink55)', textDecoration: 'none', cursor: 'pointer' },
            sepStyle: c.last ? { display: 'none' } : { color: 'var(--ink40)', padding: '0 0.5em' }
          }));
        }
        const picks = st.picks || [];
        if (picks.length === 1) {
          const parts = String(picks[0]).split(':');
          return this.crumbTrail('category', { catGroup: parts[0], catValue: parts.slice(1).join(':') });
        }
        // Multi-facet keeps the long /browse form, which the build marks
        // noindex — so the trail stops at Browse rather than naming one of
        // several values and implying it is the page.
        if (picks.length > 1) return this.crumbTrail('category', { catGroup: 'Browse' });
        // A committed pick with no facet list still has a category and a value
        // in resultCat / resultLabel — the same pair the chip prints.
        if (st.resultCat && st.resultLabel) return this.crumbTrail('category', { catGroup: st.resultCat, catValue: st.resultLabel });
        return this.crumbTrail('category', { catGroup: 'Browse' });
      })(),
      catCrumbsStyle: setOpen ? this.crumbRowStyle() : { display: 'none' },
      catInfo: setOpen
        // The filtered count is DATA too — it read a literal '12 of 179' while the
        // unfiltered branch beside it read TITLES. Both now read the table; the
        // site supplies the filtered figure under TITLES.filtered[1] (mock: same
        // window as releases) so a browse pick never shows a number the drop typed.
        ? (siteName + ' · ' + (st.resultCat || '') + ' · ' + this.countLine('filtered', this.shownCount('filtered')))
        : (siteName + ' · ' + this.countLine('releases', this.shownCount('releases'))),
      // THE FACET, AS A REMOVABLE CHIP. A browse pick filters this grid rather
      // than throwing you into the star field, so the grid has to say what it is
      // filtered by and offer a way out. Clearing it drops back to the full
      // catalogue and resets the star view with it, so the two modes cannot
      // disagree about what is being shown.
      catChipStyle: setOpen
        ? { marginTop: '14px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '9px', width: 'max-content',
            height: '30px', padding: '0 6px 0 11px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '15px',
            border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', background: 'rgba(219,191,61,0.08)',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9.5px', letterSpacing: '0.12em',
            textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // "Constellation · <artist>" for the ⤢ Open path, the pick's own name for
      // a browse pick. The category is part of the label here because a
      // constellation and a genre are different kinds of set and the chip is
      // the only place that is said (2026-09-05).
      // ── ASK 1 · BESIDE THE COUNT, NOT THE CHIP (2026-09-05) ───────────────
      // The two ends of that row do different jobs: the chip is how you take a
      // facet OFF, and the count is the answer to the view as it stands. What
      // you share is the view, so the control belongs with the count — and
      // putting it beside the chip would sit "copy this" next to "remove
      // this", which is a bad neighbourhood for an irreversible-looking act.
      //
      // Same affordance as the episode and track menus: emits share.copy and
      // shows "✓ Link copied" for two seconds. The clipboard write is the
      // host's (the design has never carried one) — and per the housekeeping
      // note it should prefer the canonical short form, which for a single
      // pick is /genre/<value> rather than the /browse query.
      catCopyLabel: st.copied ? '✓ Link copied' : '⧉ Copy link',
      catCopyStyle: setOpen
        ? {
            display: 'inline-flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '22px', padding: '0 8px',

            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px',
            letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
            cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap'
          }
        : { display: 'none' },
      catCopy: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        const picks = st.picks || [];
        this.emit('share.copy', {
          source: 'results',
          // What the host should put on the clipboard: the canonical short form
          // for a single pick, the long browse form for a multi-facet view,
          // and the constellation path when that is what is open.
          canonical: st.conAnchor ? 'constellation' : (picks.length === 1 ? 'category' : (picks.length > 1 ? 'browse' : null)),
          picks: picks,
          artist: st.conAnchor || null
        });
        this.setState({ copied: true });
        setTimeout(() => this.setState({ copied: false }), 2000);
      },
      catChipLabel: st.conAnchor
        ? ('Constellation · ' + st.conAnchor)
        : (st.resultLabel || ''),
      // ── DQ-59 · THE MULTI-FACET RESULT VIEW (2026-09-04) ──────────────────
      // browseFilter's own note was right: there was no multi-facet view, and
      // the single `browseSel` chip above could only ever describe one pick. A
      // shareable genre AND mood AND format link needs the view to exist, so
      // here it is, and the rule is drawn rather than implied:
      //
      //   WITHIN a category the values are a UNION — "Ambient or Drone" is one
      //   condition, so the chips of one group sit in one enclosure joined by
      //   "or", and removing one leaves the others standing.
      //   ACROSS categories it is an INTERSECTION — the groups are separated by
      //   a standing "and" that is type, not a chip, because it is the
      //   structure of the query and not something you can remove.
      //
      // Every value carries its own ✕; every GROUP carries one too, which is
      // the difference between "not ambient" and "any genre" and is a distinct
      // thing to want. Clear all is at the end, always last, never first.
      // The bar is the crawl path's visible form as well: what it reads is what
      // the URL says.
      facetGroups: (() => {
        const picks = (st.picks || []).slice();
        if (!picks.length) return [];
        const order = this.catRows().map(r => r[0]);
        const byGroup = {};
        picks.forEach(p => {
          const parts = String(p).split(':');
          const g = parts[0], v = parts.slice(1).join(':');
          (byGroup[g] = byGroup[g] || []).push({ pick: p, value: v });
        });
        const names = Object.keys(byGroup).sort((a, b) => order.indexOf(a) - order.indexOf(b));
        return names.map((g, gi) => {
          const row = this.catRows().filter(r => r[0] === g)[0];
          const colour = row ? row[3] : 'var(--gold)';
          return {
            key: 'fg' + g,
            group: g,
            // The join sits BEFORE every group but the first, so the row reads
            // "Genres … and Moods … and Formats …" left to right.
            join: gi === 0 ? '' : 'and',
            joinStyle: gi === 0
              ? { display: 'none' }
              : { flexShrink: 0, padding: '0 2px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.18em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' },
            box: {
              display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '7px', flexWrap: 'wrap',
              padding: '5px 8px 5px 10px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '6px',
              border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', background: 'var(--tile)'
            },
            nameStyle: { flexShrink: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '6px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
            dot: { width: '7px', height: '7px', borderRadius: '50%', background: colour, flexShrink: 0 },
            // One ✕ for the whole category.
            clearStyle: { flexShrink: 0, marginLeft: '2px', width: '18px', height: '18px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', borderRadius: '3px', fontSize: '9px', color: 'var(--ink40)', cursor: 'pointer' },
            clear: () => this.setState({ picks: (this.state.picks || []).filter(x => String(x).split(':')[0] !== g) }),
            values: byGroup[g].map((v, vi) => ({
              key: 'fv' + v.pick,
              label: v.value,
              // "or" between values inside the group, for the same reason "and"
              // sits between groups: the reader should not have to guess which
              // way a multi-pick behaves.
              or: vi === 0 ? '' : 'or',
              orStyle: vi === 0
                ? { display: 'none' }
                : { flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'lowercase', color: 'var(--ink40)' },
              chip: {
                display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '6px', height: '26px',
                padding: '0 4px 0 9px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px',
                background: 'var(--bg)', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
                fontSize: '12px', color: 'var(--ink)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap'
              },
              xStyle: { width: '18px', height: '18px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', borderRadius: '3px', fontSize: '9px', color: 'var(--ink40)', cursor: 'pointer' },
              remove: () => this.setState({ picks: (this.state.picks || []).filter(x => x !== v.pick) })
            }))
          };
        });
      })(),
      facetBarStyle: (st.picks || []).length
        ? { marginTop: '14px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: '8px' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // The count is the answer to the query, so it belongs on the same row as
      // the query. FILTER_COUNT when the site supplies it; the painted window
      // otherwise, per § 10.
      facetCount: (() => {
        if (!(st.picks || []).length) return '';
        const fc = this.data('FILTER_COUNT');
        const n = (typeof fc === 'number') ? fc : this.shownCount('filtered');
        return n + (n === 1 ? ' record' : ' records');
      })(),
      facetCountStyle: { flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
      facetClearAllStyle: (st.picks || []).length > 1
        ? { flexShrink: 0, marginLeft: '4px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      facetClearAll: () => this.setState({ picks: [] }),
      catChipXStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
        width: '18px', height: '18px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '50%',
        background: 'rgba(219,191,61,0.18)', fontSize: '10px', cursor: 'pointer'
      },
      // Clears whichever kind of set is open — the anchor as well as the pick,
      // or ⤢ Open's grid could not be dismissed (2026-09-05).
      clearCatFilter: () => this.setState({ browseSel: null, conAnchor: null, constellation: false, resultLabel: null, resultCat: null, view: 'releases' }),
      catGridStyle: {
        marginTop: '20px', display: 'grid',
        gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(' + catCols + ', minmax(0, 1fr))',
        gap: '22px 18px'
      },
      // The same painted list the drawer and the count line use — see
      // `painted` above. No placeholder tiles (ruling 1).
      catCards: painted.map((p, i) => {
        const ph = !p[0];
        const live = st.catHover === i;
        const isPlaying = st.npPlay === i;
        // ── DQ-55 · THE LABEL LINE, KEYED ON THE SET (2026-09-05) ────────────
        // A card names its label when the painted set contains more than
        // one — never when the set is one label's own, where the label is
        // already the chrome, the header and the domain. Keyed on the SET and
        // not the route, exactly as DQ-55 says, so it is on for the portal
        // category page, a mixed search and the group catalogue, and off on a
        // label browsing its own records. The distinct count is computed from
        // what is painted, so no surface has to declare its own scope.
        const labelOf = (row) => ((this.data('PLACE')[row && row[0]] || [])[2]) || '';
        const spread = painted.map(labelOf).filter((v, k, a) => v && a.indexOf(v) === k).length > 1;
        const mine = labelOf(p);
        return {
          key: 'cc' + i,
          // §1a · A DESTINATION THAT EXISTS IN THE DOCUMENT. This card was the
          // measured case — a releases page of 1,310 cursor:pointer divs and
          // zero anchors. `open` still does exactly what it did.
          // The two data- attributes are the resolver hook for the one handle I
          // cannot derive safely: see addr('album') on why 'S/T' collides.
          // §2 of r47 · the ROW, so addr() reads its supplied address rather than
          // deriving one. The two data- attributes stay: they are how you resolve

          href: ph ? null : this.addr('album', p),

          albumTitle: ph ? '' : (p[1] || ''),
          labelName: spread && mine ? mine : '',

            ? { marginTop: '4px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' }
            : { display: 'none' },
          card: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', minWidth: 0 },
          cover: {

          },
          // Hover swaps in the ALTERNATE image — PAIRS index 4, usually the
          // merch shot. Held in its own layer that cross-fades over the cover,
          // so the swap reads as a turn rather than a reload; a record with no
          // alt on file just keeps its cover.
          altStyle: {
            position: 'absolute', inset: 0,
            opacity: (live && !ph && p[4]) ? 1 : 0,
            transition: 'opacity 260ms ease',
            background: p[4] ? 'url(' + this.res(p[4]) + ') center/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)' : 'transparent',
            pointerEvents: 'none'
          },
          // The cover is a door, the ▶ is a button: it appears lower-left on
          // hover and plays in place rather than navigating.
          //
          // AND IT HOLDS ITS STATE (2026-09-01). Two things were wrong. It only
          // existed while `live` — so the button for the playing record
          // disappeared as soon as the cursor moved, and the grid could not tell
          // you what was playing. And it was always ▶, even while playing, so
          // pressing it again had no visible meaning. Now: the PLAYING card's
          // button is always present (hover or not) and reads ❙❙; pressing it
          // pauses, which returns the glyph to ▶ while the card keeps the
          // button. Every other card behaves exactly as before — ▶ on hover,
          // nothing at rest.
          // ══ ON A PHONE THE ▶︎ IS PERSISTENT (2026-09-14, audit) ═══════════
          // ⚠ THIS WAS THE COST OF REMOVING HOVER, AND IT MADE THE PHONE GRID
          // UNPLAYABLE. The gate was `(live || isPlaying) && !ph`, and `live`
          // is `st.catHover === i` — a HOVER state. On a desktop that is right:
          // the ▶︎ is revealed by the cursor, so 200 covers stay quiet. On a
          // phone there is no cursor, so nothing ever revealed it: every card
          // computed display:none, and with hover audio gone too a visitor had
          // NO way to start a record from the catalogue. Player is the phone's
          // one audio mode, and the grid is its main surface — "grid view with
          // player audio on it" was the whole point of the simplification.
          //
          // So on narrow the control is simply THERE. That is a deliberate
          // reversal of the quiet-cover rule, not an oversight of it: the rule
          // existed to keep a SECONDARY control off 200 covers (the ⋮ still
          // obeys it), and play is not secondary here — it is the primary act
          // the page exists for. A revealed control needs something to reveal
          // it, and a phone has nothing.
          //
          // 44px on narrow, 40 on desktop: CLAUDE.md's target rule applies to
          // the device that actually uses fingers.
          playStyle: this.playCtl(((narrow || live || isPlaying) && !ph)
            ? { position: 'absolute', left: '10px', bottom: '10px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: narrow ? '44px' : '40px', height: narrow ? 'var(--ink40)' : 'var(--ongold)', fontSize: '12px', cursor: p[5] === false ? 'default' : 'pointer', boxShadow: p[5] === false ? 'none' : '0 2px 10px rgba(0,0,0,0.35)' }
            : { display: 'none' }),
          // V2 · no ▶︎ on a cover in hover mode — the cover IS the control there.
          // ❙❙ still shows while this record is the one sounding, because that is
          // state rather than an affordance.
          playGlyph: (isPlaying && !st.paused) ? '❙❙' : (this.playMode(st) ? '▶︎' : ''),
          // A hairline gold ring on the cover of the record that is playing, so
          // the state survives even where the button is a 40px square in the
          // corner of a 5-across grid.
          playRingStyle: isPlaying
            ? { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, borderRadius: '7px', border: '1px solid var(--gold)', pointerEvents: 'none' }
            : { display: 'none' },
          capRow: { marginTop: '11px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'flex-start', gap: '6px' },
          title: ph ? 'from catalogue' : p[1],
          // The title steps back so the two caption lines do not compete: with the
          // artist raised to full ink, a 500-weight title beside it made both
          // lines shout. Artist strong, title secondary — the same pairing the
          // drawer row and the ATH column row now use.
          titleStyle: {
            flex: 1, minWidth: 0, fontSize: '12.5px', fontWeight: 400,
            letterSpacing: '-0.005em', lineHeight: 1.2, color: 'var(--ink70)',
            fontStyle: ph ? 'italic' : 'normal', textWrap: 'pretty'
          },
          dotsStyle: ph
            ? { display: 'none' }
            : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: narrow ? '44px' : '22px', height: narrow ? '44px' : '22px', flexShrink: 0, marginRight: narrow ? '-10px' : '-4px', fontSize: '15px', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },

        artistStyle: { marginTop: '-1px', fontSize: '11.5px', fontWeight: ph ? 400 : 500, lineHeight: 1.3, color: ph ? 'var(--ink55)' : 'var(--ink)', fontStyle: ph ? 'italic' : 'normal' },
          enter: () => { if (!ph) this.hoverEnter({ catHover: i }); },
          leave: () => this.setState({ catHover: null }),
          // ── THE METER ON THE COVER (2026-09-10, Tom) ──────────────────────
          // "In grid mode the covers need an audio visualiser on them, on the
          // cover itself." It is the clearest statement hover mode can make:
          // the artwork under your pointer is the thing making the sound.
          //
          // Bottom-left, inside the cover, on a dark plate — a cover is a
          // PHOTOGRAPH, so the bars carry the literal gold ladder and the plate
          // guarantees contrast on a pale sleeve. The play ring and the ▶ are
          // centred, so this corner is free in both modes.
          // ⚠ A SQUARE PLATE, AND THE BARS LIVE INSIDE IT (2026-09-10, Tom:

          // It was 35 × 21 — a wide lozenge holding an 11px ladder that
          // reached the plate's top edge, so five gold bars stood on a dark

          // plate was not square and the ladder had no headroom.
          //
          // 30 × 30 with the meter standing on a baseline 7px up: the ladder
          // is 4→18, so at full signal 4px of plate still shows above the
          // tallest bar and the bars are contained at every level rather than
          // topping out flush. Fixed height and width, so the plate does not
          // resize with the signal — a meter that changes shape reads as a
          // layout bug, which is half of what made this one look wrong.
          vuStyle: this.hoverVuBox(st, !ph && st.catHover === i, {
            position: 'absolute', left: '8px', bottom: '8px', zIndex: 3,

          // §1 of r47 · navTo(), which was carried to the nav rows and the artist
          // tiles and NOT to this one. You reproduced it: a plain left-click on a
          // cover left defaultPrevented false, the document began unloading, and
          // the design re-booted from zero. My own comment on navTo() says it
          // stops exactly that. Nothing else about the click changes.
          open: this.navTo(() => { if (!ph) this.openRecord(i); }),
          // Same act as the landing hero's ▶: the transport arrives and the page
          // contracts above it. Pressing the PLAYING card's button pauses
          // instead of restarting — the button is the record's state, not a
          // second way to trigger it.
          // hasAudio (PAIRS[i][5], optional, §64): false → this record has no
          // preview; its ▶ is dimmed and inert and never becomes `rec`.
          noAudio: p[5] === false,
          // ══ preventDefault, NOT JUST stopPropagation (2026-09-14) ═══════
          // ⚠ THIS IS THE "HANG" I SHIPPED r76 UNABLE TO EXPLAIN, AND IT WAS
          // NEVER A HANG — IT WAS A NAVIGATION. Engineering measured it in
          // headless Chromium: the click navigates to /album/<slug> and the
          // document unloads.
          //
          // This span sits INSIDE the cover anchor. stopPropagation() stops the
          // bubble to c.open — and c.open is navTo(), which is the ONLY thing
          // that calls preventDefault(). So the anchor's default was never
          // cancelled, the browser followed href, and the design re-booted from
          // zero. From inside the tool that is indistinguishable from a hang,
          // and it explains every piece of evidence I gathered: renderVals
          // really did complete, the sessionStorage marker really did survive
          // (it survives an unload), and nothing after the commit ever happened
          // because THERE WAS NO AFTER.
          //
          // My five killed hypotheses were all killed correctly. The cause was
          // outside the render entirely, and I never tested for an unload —
          // which is the one thing "the page stopped responding" should have
          // suggested.
          //
          // ⚠ AND IT IS THE r47 §1 DEFECT REINTRODUCED AT THE PLAY CONTROL.
          // The comment twenty lines above this one describes it exactly: "a
          // plain left-click on a cover left defaultPrevented false, the
          // document began unloading, and the design re-booted from zero."
          // The lesson is narrow and worth stating: INSIDE AN ANCHOR,
          // stopPropagation() IS NOT ENOUGH. It stops the handler that would
          // have cancelled the default; it does not stop the default.
          //
          // It masqueraded as "only on a phone" because the persistent ▶︎ (§4
          // of r76) is what let a phone reach a handler that has been live in
          // production since r75 — byte-identical in public/dc/catalogue.dc.html.
          play: (e) => {
            if (e) {
              if (e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
              if (e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
            }
            if (ph || p[5] === false) return;
            this.play(i);
          },
          // Same anchor, same exposure — openCtx stops the bubble but cannot
          // cancel the anchor's default either. Wrapped rather than changing
          // openCtx, which is called from twelve places, most outside anchors.
          dots: (e) => {
            if (e && e.preventDefault) e.preventDefault();
            return this.openCtx('catalogue', i)(e);
          }
        };
      }),

      // ── Radio · 7n / 7r ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

          : { width: '7px', height: '7px', borderRadius: '50%', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)' },
        badge: { flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' }
      })),
      radioNoteStyle: { padding: '14px 16px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', border: '1px dashed var(--hair20)', fontSize: '12px', lineHeight: 1.6, color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      radioNote: 'A live stream has no duration, so the transport shows the meter and a pause and nothing else. Every record played is a record you can add — that is the whole argument for the queue living in the bar.',

      // ── Tour · 7m / 7q ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      isTourPage: route === 'tour',
      tourPadStyle: { padding: narrow ? '22px 14px 30px' : '36px 26px 40px', boxSizing: 'border-box' },
      tourHeadStyle: {
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: narrow ? 'column' : 'row',
        alignItems: narrow ? 'flex-start' : 'flex-end', gap: narrow ? '14px' : '20px',
        justifyContent: 'space-between', paddingBottom: '16px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)'
      },
      tourTitleStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: narrow ? '22px' : '30px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.02em', lineHeight: 1.05, color: 'var(--ink)' },
      // ── THE FILTER ROW SCROLLS ON A PHONE (2026-09-07) ────────────────────
      // It wrapped, and wrapping is what made it look broken: five chips of
      // uneven width at 390 fell into two ragged rows with EU orphaned on the
      // second, and each chip was narrow enough that "NORTHERN SPY 3" broke
      // INSIDE it — the label on one line and its count under it. A filter row
      // is one list of peers, so it reads as one line and scrolls; that is also
      // the easier gesture, since the chips sit under the thumb.
      // The scrollbar is hidden the way every other scroller in this file hides
      // it, and -webkit-overflow-scrolling gives it the platform's own momentum.
      tourFiltersStyle: narrow
        ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '6px', flexWrap: 'nowrap',
            marginTop: '14px', overflowX: 'auto', overflowY: 'hidden',
            scrollbarWidth: 'none', WebkitOverflowScrolling: 'touch',
            paddingBottom: '2px',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase' }
        : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '6px', flexWrap: 'wrap',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      // COUNTS ARE COUNTED (2026-09-04). This read "14" for the label and "82"
      // for the group no matter how many dates existed. Both come off the TOUR
      // rows now — the label's own by artist membership, the group's as the
      // whole table.
      tourFilters: (() => {
        // Artist index differs by table: TOUR carries the artist at [0], the
        // legacy month tables at [2]. Getting that wrong assigned the group
        // total to the label's own chip, so "Northern Spy 10" counted SUSS and
        // Shilpa Ray. Where an artist has no PLACE row their label is unknown,
        // so the label chip shows NO number rather than a wrong one.
        const T = this.data('TOUR') || [];
        const legacy = (this.data('TOUR_APRIL') || []).concat(this.data('TOUR_MAY') || []);
        const rows = T.length ? T.map(r => r[0]) : legacy.map(r => r[2]);
        const all = rows.length;
        const known = rows.filter(n => !!(this.data('PLACE')[n] || [])[2]).length;
        const mine = rows.filter(n => (this.data('PLACE')[n] || [])[2] === LB[0]).length;
        return [LB[0] + (known ? ' ' + mine : ''), 'All five labels' + (all ? ' ' + all : ''), 'US', 'EU'];
      })().map((f, i) => {
        const on = (st.tourFilter == null ? 0 : st.tourFilter) === i;
        return {
          key: 'tf' + i, label: f,
          box: {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px',
            // nowrap and shrink-proof: in the scrolling row a chip keeps its own
            // width instead of being squeezed until its label breaks in two.
            whiteSpace: 'nowrap', flexShrink: 0,
            padding: '0 13px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px', cursor: 'pointer',
            marginLeft: i === 2 ? '8px' : 0,
            background: on ? 'var(--tile)' : 'transparent',
            border: on ? '1px solid transparent' : '1px solid var(--hair20)',
            color: on ? 'var(--ink)' : 'var(--ink55)'
          },
          click: () => this.setState({ tourFilter: i })
        };
      }),
      tourCalStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', padding: '0 14px',
        whiteSpace: 'nowrap', flexShrink: 0,
        marginLeft: narrow ? 0 : '8px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px',
        border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer'
      },
      tourGridStyle: { marginTop: '26px', display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: narrow ? '1fr' : '1fr 320px', gap: narrow ? '26px' : '34px' },
      tourRailStyle: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '12px' },
      tourMonths: (() => {
        // [num, dow, artist, venue, note, photo]
        // ── THE LABEL TOUR PAGE READS THE SAME TOUR TABLE (2026-09-04) ───────
        // TOUR_APRIL / TOUR_MAY are two fixed months, which is why this page
        // could not survive June. When the artist-keyed TOUR table is supplied
        // it is grouped here by the month token that leads each date — any
        // number of months, in the order they arrive — and the two legacy
        // tables remain the fallback so nothing breaks mid-migration.
        const T = this.data('TOUR') || [];
        const MONTHS = { jan: 'January', feb: 'February', mar: 'March', apr: 'April', may: 'May', jun: 'June', jul: 'July', aug: 'August', sep: 'September', oct: 'October', nov: 'November', dec: 'December' };
        const grouped = (() => {
          if (!T.length) return null;
          const order = [], bag = {};
          T.forEach(r => {
            const parts = String(r[1] || '').trim().split(/\s+/);
            const tok = (parts.find(p => MONTHS[p.slice(0, 3).toLowerCase()]) || '').slice(0, 3).toLowerCase();
            const name = MONTHS[tok] || 'Dates';
            const day = parts.filter(p => /^\d+$/.test(p))[0] || '';
            if (!bag[name]) { bag[name] = []; order.push(name); }
            // Into the month tables' own row shape, so mk() is untouched.
            //
            // ── THE ROW'S PHOTO (2026-09-05) ────────────────────────────────
            // The 6th element is the row's photo and this line filled it with
            // '', so every row built from the injected TOUR table fell back to
            // the label mark — the slot was there and nothing ever reached it.
            // TOUR[6] is the artist's press photo. portraitsFor(artist) is the
            // fallback rather than a second source: it is the one portrait
            // reader every other surface uses, so a row and that artist's own
            // page cannot show different faces. Empty still means the mark,
            // which stays the honest answer for an artist with no photo.
            const photo = r[6] || (this.portraitsFor(r[0])[0] || {}).src || '';
            // r[5] is TOUR's ticketUrl and was being discarded here, so the
            // Tickets chip could not have been bound even if someone tried
            // (2026-09-09, code audit 2c). Carried as the 7th element.
            bag[name].push([day, '', r[0], (r[2] || '') + (r[3] ? ' \u00b7 ' + r[3] : ''), r[4] || '', photo, r[5] || '']);
          });
          return order.map(name => ({ name: name, rows: bag[name] }));
        })();

        const MAY = this.data('TOUR_MAY');
        const mk = (dates) => dates.map((d, i) => ({
          key: 'td' + d[0] + i,
          num: d[0], dow: d[1], artist: d[2], venue: d[3], // Restored on phone (2026-09-10, Tom). It is the row's only editorial line —
        // "with Lonnie Holley", "sold out" — and a phone visitor deciding whether
        // to go needs it more than a desktop one browsing.
        note: d[4],
          row: {
            display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: narrow ? '12px' : '20px',
            padding: '15px 0', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)'
          },
          dayWrap: { width: narrow ? '40px' : '58px', flexShrink: 0, textAlign: 'center' },
          numStyle: { display: 'block', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: narrow ? '17px' : '20px', color: 'var(--gold)' },
          dowStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '2px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
          thumb: {
            width: '46px', height: '46px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '7px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            display: narrow ? 'none' : 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
            border: d[5] ? 'none' : '1px solid var(--hair20)',
            background: d[5] ? 'url(' + this.res(d[5]) + ') center 20%/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)' : 'transparent'
          },
          // No press photo: the label's own mark stands in rather than a gap.
          markStyle: d[5] ? { display: 'none' } : {
            display: 'block', width: '30px', height: '30px', backgroundColor: 'var(--ink40)',
            WebkitMaskImage: 'url(' + this.res(LB[1]) + ')', maskImage: 'url(' + this.res(LB[1]) + ')',
            WebkitMaskRepeat: 'no-repeat', maskRepeat: 'no-repeat',
            WebkitMaskPosition: 'center', maskPosition: 'center',
            WebkitMaskSize: LB[2], maskSize: LB[2]
          },
          artistStyle: { display: 'block', fontSize: narrow ? '14px' : '16px', fontWeight: 500, color: 'var(--ink)' },
          venueStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '3px', fontSize: narrow ? '12px' : '13px', color: 'var(--ink70)' },
    // ⚠ THE DATA CAME BACK AND THE STYLE DID NOT (2026-09-10). Restoring
      // `note: d[4]` put "with SUSS" and "record release" into the DOM on a
      // phone while this key kept them display:none — present in the markup,
      // invisible on the page, which is the worst of the three states.
      //
      // AND A STYLE ALONE COULD NOT FIX IT: this span is a FLEX SIBLING of the
      // artist/venue column, so display:block still lays it out beside them and
      // a 390px row has no width to spare. The note needs a different PLACE on
      // a phone, not a different size — so the desktop chip stays exactly as it
      // was and notePhoneStyle carries a third line inside the text column,
      // where artist and venue already stack.
      noteStyle: narrow ? { display: 'none' } : { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', flexShrink: 0 },
      notePhoneStyle: !narrow ? { display: 'none' } : { display: 'block', marginTop: '3px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8.5px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
          // ABSENT WHERE THERE IS NO TICKET (2026-09-09). The chip was drawn on
          // every date with cursor:pointer and no handler; the same affordance is
          // an anchor on ath-artist and on the record page, so this is the third
          // copy of one control finally reading the same rule as the other two.
          ticketHref: d[6] || null,
          ticketStyle: d[6] ? {
            padding: narrow ? '10px 11px' : '12px 15px', minHeight: narrow ? '44px' : 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '5px',
            border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', cursor: 'pointer',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink)'
          } : { display: 'none' }
        }));
        const head = (first) => ({
          display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '14px', marginTop: first ? 0 : '26px',
          fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', letterSpacing: '0.2em',
          textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)'
        });
        const count = (rows) => rows.length + (rows.length === 1 ? ' date' : ' dates');
        if (grouped) {
          return grouped.map((g, i) => ({
            key: 'tm' + i, name: g.name, count: count(g.rows), headStyle: head(i === 0), dates: mk(g.rows)
          }));
        }
        return [
          { key: 'apr', name: 'April', count: count(APRIL), headStyle: head(true), dates: mk(APRIL) },
          { key: 'may', name: 'May', count: count(MAY), headStyle: head(false), dates: mk(MAY) }
        ];
      })(),
      tourCities: this.data('TOUR_CITIES')
        .map((c, i) => ({ key: 'tc' + i, city: c[0], n: c[1] })),
      // ── Album · 7c ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // The record as its own page. The sidebar view stays as the star column's
      // reading of the same record; this is the one you can link to.
      // ── The record's own data, ported from AlbumPageV2 + Sidebar §07 ──────
      recSecHeadStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '14px', marginBottom: '14px',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.2em',

      },
      recPhotoNoteStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink40)', flexShrink: 0 },

      // It used to be two file paths typed here — Jeremy Cunningham and Dave
      // Harrington — cycling on tap whoever's record was open, with a bio
      // written in prose beside them. Every value in this block now hangs off
      // ONE name: recWho = PAIRS[rec][0], the artist of the record on screen.
      //   photo   portraitsFor(recWho)  — PHOTOS[name], then INDEX[name][1]
      //   count   the real number of press shots, not "/ 2"
      //   bio     ATH_BIOS[name] → PLACE[name][0] → nothing on the site
      //   people  REC_CONTRIBUTORS, narrowed to this record when its rows say
      //   dates   tourFor(recWho) — never an unfiltered tour table
      //   videos  videosFor(recWho, title) — real urls and posters
      recPhotoNote: (() => {
        const n = this.portraitsFor(pair ? pair[0] : '').length;
        return n > 1 ? ('portrait ' + (((st.recPhoto || 0) % n) + 1) + ' / ' + n) : (n === 1 ? '1 press photo' : 'no photo on file');
      })(),
      cycleArtistPhoto: () => {
        const n = this.portraitsFor(pair ? pair[0] : '').length;
        if (n < 2) return;
        this.setState({ recPhoto: ((this.state.recPhoto || 0) + 1) % n });
      },
      recArtistPhotoStyle: (() => {
        const set = this.portraitsFor(pair ? pair[0] : '');
        if (!set.length) this.missing('PHOTOS', 'record', (pair ? pair[0] : ''));
        const shot = set.length ? set[(st.recPhoto || 0) % set.length] : null;
        return {
          width: narrow ? '96px' : '132px', height: narrow ? '96px' : '132px', flexShrink: 0,
          borderRadius: '7px', boxSizing: 'border-box', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
          cursor: set.length > 1 ? 'pointer' : 'default',
          // No photo for this artist: the hatch says so. It must never fall
          // back to another artist's portrait or to the record's cover.
          background: shot
            ? ('url(' + this.res(shot.src) + ') center ' + (shot.focus || '20%') + '/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)')
            : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 8px, transparent 8px 16px)'
        };
      })(),
      recBio: (() => {
        // BIO SOURCE, RULED 2026-09-04: Album.artistBio (Shopify `artist.bio`),
        // newest record first, decoded by artistPage.decodeArtistBio — injected
        // here as ATH_BIOS[name]. NO SCENE FALLBACK: a scene is a placement,
        // not a biography, and it was ruled out. No bio → nothing on the site.
        const who = pair ? pair[0] : '';
        const real = this.artistBio(who) || '';
        if (real) return real;
        this.missing('ATH_BIOS', 'record', who);
        return isSite ? '' : 'Biography pending — the artist page carries the full note, discography and press shots.';

      // one face, and being on two records is not a reason for two photos.

        const mine = tagged.length && pair ? tagged.filter(c => c[2] === this.recKey(pair)) : rows;

        },

      }));
      })(),
      // The block goes away when this record names nobody — an "On tour" rule

      recTourBlockStyle: this.tourFor(pair ? pair[0] : '').length
        ? { marginTop: '30px', paddingTop: '22px', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)' }
        : (this.missing('TOUR', 'record', (pair ? pair[0] : '')), { display: 'none' }),
      // TourEvent extras — eventName, endDate, isHeadliner, type — badge only

      // put whatever the table held under every record, and on the site the

      recTourEvents: this.tourFor(pair ? pair[0] : '').map((e, i) => ({

        dateStyle: { width: '58px', flexShrink: 0, fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '11px', color: 'var(--gold)' },
        venueStyle: { display: 'block', fontSize: '14px', color: 'var(--ink)' },
        cityStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '2px', fontSize: '12px', color: 'var(--ink70)' },
        badgeStyle: e[3]
          ? { flexShrink: 0, padding: '4px 8px', borderRadius: '4px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' }
          : { display: 'none' },
        ticketStyle: { flexShrink: 0, padding: '9px 12px', minHeight: narrow ? '44px' : 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink)', cursor: 'pointer' }
      })),

      // The whole block, not just the grid: no videos → no "Videos" heading and

      recVideoBlockStyle: (() => {

      // No rows is not a gap to fill: say it, in the same words the artist

      recVideoEmpty: '',

        }
