      // THE COVER IS 360px, NOT THE FRAME'S 300px — Tom, 2026-08-25: "make the
      // album cover bigger by 20%". The whole object column scales with it.
      recGridStyle: { display: 'grid', gridTemplateColumns: narrow ? '1fr' : '360px minmax(0, 1fr)', gap: narrow ? '14px' : '40px', alignItems: 'start' },
      recCoverStyle: (() => {
        // THE COVER IS THE SELECTED ONE OF THE REAL SET (2026-09-04). It only
        // ever drew PAIRS[3] and hatched every alternate, so clicking a real
        // second cover blanked the frame.
        const set = this.coversFor(pair);
        const src = set.length ? set[(st.recAlt || 0) % set.length] : '';
        return {
          position: 'relative', width: narrow ? '100%' : '360px', aspectRatio: '1',
          borderRadius: '9px', boxSizing: 'border-box', border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)',
          background: src ? 'url(' + this.res(src) + ') center/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)'
            : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 8px, transparent 8px 16px)'
        };
      })(),
      // The "1 / N" badge KEEPS THE COUNT — it is what the retired pager dots
      // were carrying, and the only place the count now lives.
      // THE "1 / N" BADGE COUNTS THE REAL SET (2026-09-04). It said "/ 3"
      // because the strip below was three fixed slots. coversFor() is the set:
      // Album.featuredImage → PAIRS[3], the first Album.images entry → PAIRS[4],
      // the rest through REC_COVERS[title]. One cover reads 1 / 1.
      recCount: (() => {
        const n = this.coversFor(pair).length;
        return n ? (((st.recAlt || 0) % n) + 1) + ' / ' + n : '';
      })(),
      recCountStyle: {
        position: 'absolute', right: '10px', bottom: '10px', padding: '4px 8px', borderRadius: '4px',
        background: 'rgba(16,18,21,0.75)', color: '#f3f1ea',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.1em'
      },
      // 44px THUMBNAILS, not pager dots. The frame floats three dots 20px below
      // the cover, overlapping this strip; the thumbnails are the working
      // affordance (click to switch), so the redundant colliding dots are not
      // drawn. Strip starts 10px below the cover.
      // ONE THUMBNAIL PER COVER THE RECORD ACTUALLY HAS — not three slots with
      // two of them hatched. Hidden entirely for a single-cover release.
      recAltsRowStyle: this.coversFor(pair).length > 1
        ? { marginTop: '10px', display: 'flex', gap: '6px' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      recAlts: this.coversFor(pair).map((src, i) => {
        const on = (st.recAlt || 0) === i;
        return {
          key: 'ra' + i,
          title: i === 0 ? 'Front cover' : ('Alternate ' + i),
          box: {
            width: '44px', height: '44px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
            borderRadius: '6px', cursor: 'pointer',
            border: '1px solid ' + (on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
            background: 'url(' + this.res(src) + ') center/cover no-repeat, var(--tile)'
          },
          click: () => this.setState({ recAlt: i })
        };
      }),
      // Four facts only: catalogue number, release date, genre, format.
      // On a phone the metadata drops below the actions; on desktop it stays in
      // the cover column where it has always been.
      recMetaStyle: {
        marginTop: '14px', order: narrow ? 3 : 0,
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', lineHeight: 2, whiteSpace: 'pre-line', color: 'var(--ink55)'
      },
      // ── F2 · THE RECORD'S OWN GENRES (2026-09-04) ─────────────────────────
      // The middle line read PLACE[artist][1] — the ARTIST's union of tags —
      // so 468 of 491 records could not show their own, and a label's most
      // stylistically varied artist mislabelled every record they made.
      // RECORD_GENRES is keyed by record id (recKey). No fallback to the
      // artist's: "this record's genres" and "everything its artist has been
      // tagged" are different claims, so the line omits itself instead.
      // ── DQ-56 · THE LABEL IS A TOKEN, NOT A SUBSTRING (2026-09-05) ────────
      // I specified the label name in --ink with a --gold ↗, linking to that
      // label's own site — and then rendered the whole meta block as ONE
      // concatenated text node, so there was no token to make a link of. The
      // spec was right; the markup could not express it.
      //
      // Split now: recMetaLabel is the label alone (a real anchor to
      // siteUrl(idx)), recMeta is the catalogue number and date after it, and
      // the genre and format lines follow as before. Per DQ-56 the label is NOT
      // a link on its own domain — a link to the page you are on teaches the
      // reader the trail is decorative, which is DQ-61's point about crumbs.
      recLabelIdx: (() => {
        const head = String(pair ? pair[2] : '');
        const first = head.split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        // Only the LEADING token, and only when it names a label we know: the
        // convention is 'Label · CAT# · Date' (r5), but an unmigrated row still
        // starts with its catalogue number and must not be dressed as a link.
        return this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first);
      })(),
      recMetaLabel: (() => {
        const head = String(pair ? pair[2] : '');
        const first = head.split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        return this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first) > -1 ? first : '';
      })(),
      recMetaLabelHref: (() => {
        const head = String(pair ? pair[2] : '');
        const first = head.split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        const idx = this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first);
        // §1a · null, not '': when the record is on the label you are already
        // on there is no cross-link, and href="" would reload this very page.
        // Same rule as addr(). This anchor was one of three carrying an empty
        // href before the sweep found them.
        return (idx > -1 && idx !== st.label) ? this.siteUrl(idx) : null;
      })(),
      recMetaLabelStyle: (() => {
        const head = String(pair ? pair[2] : '');
        const first = head.split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        const idx = this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first);
        if (idx < 0) return { display: 'none' };
        const here = idx === st.label;
        return {
          color: 'var(--ink)', textDecoration: 'none',
          cursor: here ? 'default' : 'pointer',
          pointerEvents: here ? 'none' : 'auto'
        };
      })(),
      recMetaArrowStyle: (() => {
        const head = String(pair ? pair[2] : '');
        const first = head.split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        const idx = this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first);
        return (idx > -1 && idx !== st.label) ? { marginLeft: '0.35em', color: 'var(--gold)' } : { display: 'none' };
      })(),
      recMeta: (() => {
        const head = pair ? pair[2] : ((this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE)) ? '' : 'catalogue number · date pending');
        const first = String(head).split(' \u00b7 ')[0] || '';
        const idx = this.LABELS.map(l => l[0]).indexOf(first);
        // Whatever the label token did not take. The separator travels with the
        // remainder, so the line reads identically to before.
        const rest = idx > -1 ? String(head).slice(first.length) : String(head);
        const genres = (this.data('RECORD_GENRES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)] || [];
        // ── THE FORMATS ARE CHIPS, NOT A THIRD LINE (2026-09-06, Tom) ────────
        // This line ended with a formats run — LP · CS · DL — which the chips
        // beneath it now carry, and which the removed kicker was ALSO saying:
        // three statements of one fact in one view. The chips keep it, because
        // a pill reads as a thing you can buy rather than as more metadata.
        return rest + (genres.length ? '\n' + genres.join(' · ') : '');
      })(),
      // THE KICKER IS THE RECORD'S RELEASE STATE (2026-09-04). It was the
      // literal 'Out now · LP · CS · DL', which announced three formats on
      // every record and called a preorder "out now". PAIRS[5] is the optional
      // release state — 'preorder' where preorder.isPreorder(Album.releaseTs)
      // is true, anything else (or absent) means released — and the formats
      // half comes from BUY, the same source as the chips below.
      // recKicker / recKickerStyle DELETED 2026-09-06 with the duplicated
      // header. PAIRS[5]'s release state moved into recFormatChips as a pill —
      // see its note. Deleted rather than left defined: a definition with no
      // caller is what the build reads as intent, which is the rule this file
      // cites three times and the grounds on which showConstellation went in
      // r22. recCrumbs went the same way; crumbTrail() and crumbRowStyle stay,
      // because the artist and category pages still render trails.
      recTitleStyle: { order: narrow ? -2 : undefined, marginTop: '10px', fontSize: narrow ? '24px' : '34px', fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.025em', lineHeight: 1.04, color: 'var(--ink)' },
      recTitle: pair ? pair[1] : 'Record pending',
      recArtistStyle: { order: narrow ? -3 : undefined, marginTop: narrow ? '4px' : '8px', fontSize: narrow ? '14px' : '16px', color: 'var(--ink70)' },
      recArtist: pair ? pair[0] : 'artist pending',
      // ── ⤢ OPEN SITS WITH THE LABEL (2026-09-05, Tom) ──────────────────────
      // `justify-content: space-between` on a column this wide threw the
      // control to the far right edge, hundreds of pixels from the words it
      // belongs to — so on the album page it read as page chrome rather than as
      // part of the constellation block. A control's meaning comes from what it
      // is NEXT TO. The row is left-packed now: the label, then Open beside it,
      // with only the ＋ chip pushed to the trailing edge (that one is a
      // different action — add this record to MY constellations — and reads
      // correctly as a separate, trailing affordance).
      recConHeadStyle: {
        marginTop: '20px', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.2em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)'
      },

      recConNamesStyle: { marginTop: '10px', fontSize: '14px', lineHeight: 1.6, color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      // The record's own neighbours, derived from ITS artist's placements —
      // never the head of the catalogue, and never the record's own artist.
      recConNames: this.constellationFor(pair ? pair[0] : '').join('  ·  '),
      recActionsStyle: narrow
        ? { order: -1, marginTop: '14px', display: 'flex', gap: '6px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase' }
        : { marginTop: '16px', display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      // Frame 2a, per the codebase: ▶ Play · Buy: choose format ⌄ · ⋮. ＋ Add is
      // not in the row — it is the + chip on the constellation heading.
      // ── ONE TEST FOR BOTH PAGES (2026-09-04) ──────────────────────────────
      // This required `npPlay === recIdx && recIdx != null`, so on a route
      // where no record is locked the album page's label froze on "Play" for
      // the same reason ATH's did — a second resolution of "what is this page
      // about", disagreeing with the one the action uses. Both now read
      // pageSubject / subjectPlaying, which is what makes the two pages behave
      // identically rather than similarly.
      recPlayLabel: (() => {
        const subject = this.pageSubject(route, st, athArtWho);
        if (this.subjectPlaying(subject, st)) return '❙❙ Pause';
        return narrow ? '▶\uFE0E Play' : '▶\uFE0E Play release';
      })(),
      recPlayStyle: this.playCtl(narrow
        ? { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', height: '42px', padding: '0 8px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', background: 'var(--gold)', color: 'var(--ongold)', fontWeight: 700, cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }
        : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', height: '42px', padding: '0 18px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', background: 'var(--gold)', color: 'var(--ongold)', fontWeight: 700, cursor: 'pointer' }),
      recDotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '42px', height: '42px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', fontSize: '15px', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },
      recAddGlyph: st.added ? '✓' : '＋',
      addToConstellations: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ added: !st.added }); },
      recAddChipStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
        // The row is left-packed now (see recConHeadStyle), so this chip takes
        // the trailing edge itself rather than relying on space-between — which
        // was also throwing ⤢ Open out there with it (2026-09-05).
        marginLeft: 'auto',
        width: '28px', height: '28px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
        borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: '12px',
        border: '1px solid ' + (st.added ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
        background: st.added ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : 'transparent',
        color: st.added ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)'
      },
      recArtistBtnStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '42px', padding: '0 16px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer' },
      recTracks: (() => {
        // One source of truth: track one is the record's own title, so the page
        // can never name a tracklist it refuses to name the record for.
        const NAMES = this.recTrackRows(pair);
        // THE TRACKLIST IS A PLAYER, NOT A LIST (2026-09-03). `on` was hard-coded to
        // row 0 and no row had a click — the ❙❙ on track 01 was decoration. A row is
        // "on" when THIS record is playing and its trackIdx is this row; clicking a
        // row plays it (trackIdx + play(rec)), clicking the playing row pauses. The
        // transport tuple carries `track`, so the site plays the right one.
        const recPlaying = st.started && !st.paused && st.npPlay === recIdx && recIdx != null;
        const curTrack = st.npPlay === recIdx ? (st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx) : -1;
        return NAMES.map((t, i) => {
          const on = recPlaying && curTrack === i;
          // Is the cursor on THIS row? One local, read by the wash, the ink and
          // the meter, so the three cannot light different rows.
          const hovOn = st.catHover === recIdx && st.hoverTrack === i;
          return {
            key: 'rt' + i,
            // ── A TRACK ROW IS A HOVER TARGET (2026-09-09, Tom) ──────────────
            // "On album pages, the track listing — I should be able to mouse over
            // each track and hear the sample of that track." In hover mode the row
            // has no play button by design, so the hover IS the control; without
            // this the tracklist was the one surface that made no sound at all.
            //
            // catHover names the RECORD and hoverTrack names the ROW, because a
            // record is not a track: the tuple had no way to say "this one of the
            // twelve", so a host could only ever have previewed track 1.
            enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ catHover: recIdx, hoverTrack: i, catHoverName: (t[3] || (pair ? pair[0] : '')), catHoverTitle: t[0] }),
          // ── THE METER BESIDE THE TRACK NAME (2026-09-10, Tom) ───────────
          // "In tracklists it needs to be a visualiser next to the track name,
          // so you can see right where your mouse is." On THIS row only —
          // catHover names the record and hoverTrack names the row, so the
          // meter cannot appear on a row the cursor is not on, which is the
          // whole point of putting it here instead of in the header.
          vuStyle: this.hoverVuBox(st, hovOn, { marginLeft: '9px' }),
          vuBars: this.hoverVuBars(5),
            leave: () => this.setState({ catHover: null, hoverTrack: null, catHoverName: null, catHoverTitle: null }),
            click: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); if (recIdx == null) return; if (on) { this.setState({ paused: true }); return; } this.play(recIdx, i); },
            // The row states what it does: a press in player mode, a preview in
          // hover mode (2026-09-09). cursor and title both follow the mode —
          // a pointer over a row whose click play() refuses is a button that
          // is not there.
          rowTitle: this.playMode(st) ? 'Play this track' : 'Preview — hover to hear',
          // ── THE ROW LIGHTS UNDER THE CURSOR (2026-09-10, Tom: "have it
          // highlight the track and have the visualizer so it's really
          // obvious") ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
          // The meter alone is 15px of movement in a 1fr row; the wash is what
          // makes the whole line read as the one that is sounding.
          //
          // ⚠ IT IS THE SAME GOLD AS PLAYING, ONE STEP DOWN — 0.05 against the
          // playing row's 0.1, and the ink still goes gold. Hovering and
          // playing are the same event in hover mode (the preview IS the
          // playback), so they must not be two different colours; what
          // separates them is weight, not hue.
          //
          // ⚠ AND THE ROW HAS TO STAY BOUNDED. It is a flex row with no
          // radius and full-bleed edges, so the wash gets negative margins and
          // matching padding — otherwise the highlight stops short of the
          // panel's gutters and reads as a stray band rather than as the row.
          row: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px',
            padding: '10px 10px', margin: '0 -10px', borderRadius: '5px',
            borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)',
            background: hovOn ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.05)' : 'transparent',
            transition: 'background 120ms linear',
            cursor: this.playMode(st) ? 'pointer' : 'default',
            color: (on || hovOn) ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink)' },
            num: '0' + (i + 1),
            numStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink40)' },
            glyph: on ? '❙❙' : (this.playMode(st) ? '▶︎' : ''),
            // V2 · THE CELL GOES, NOT JUST THE GLYPH. I emptied the glyph string
            // in hover mode and left this style unconditional, so every row kept
            // a 16px empty column where the ▶︎ had been — the ATH rows hide
            // theirs and the album rows did not, which is the tell. An invisible
            // control still occupying its column is the same class of fault as
            // the —:— timestamp: it holds the space and says nothing.
            //
            // ❙❙ SURVIVES: when this row is the one sounding, the cell is state
            // rather than an affordance, and in hover mode the sound is exactly
            // what the visitor caused by moving here.
            glyphStyle: on
              ? { width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', color: 'var(--gold)' }
              : this.playCtl({ width: '16px', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '10px', color: 'var(--ink40)' }),
            name: t[0],
            nameStyle: { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, fontSize: '13px', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
            // THE TRACK'S OWN CREDIT (2026-09-04, r5 ask 6). This printed the
            // record's artist on every row — wrong on the two Split records,
            // where the tracks belong to different artists. REC_TRACKS[3] is
            // the per-track credit; absent → the record's artist.
            // Restored on phone (2026-09-10, Tom): a per-track credit is exactly the
            // "never a title without its artist" rule at track level.
            who: (t[3] || (pair ? pair[0] : '')),
            whoStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: narrow ? '9px' : '10px', minWidth: 0, overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', flexShrink: 1, color: on ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.8)' : 'var(--ink55)' },
            time: t[1] || '',
            timeStyle: t[1]
              ? { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '10px', flexShrink: 0, color: on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)' }
              : { display: 'none' },
            // The album page's tracklist ⋮ had a style and no handler, so it
            // read as interactive and did nothing — the same defect class as the
            // sidebar tracklist's, which was fixed and this one was missed. Same
            // menu, same kind: openCtx stops propagation, so pressing it cannot
            // also fire the row and start the track.
            ctx: this.openCtx('track', recIdx),
            dotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', margin: '-6px -6px -6px 0', flexShrink: 0, fontSize: '13px', cursor: 'pointer', color: 'var(--ink70)' }
          };
        });
      })(),
      recTrackFootStyle: { paddingTop: '10px', borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair)', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'space-between', gap: '10px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' },
      // "N tracks" — and · M min ONLY where durations exist. Ruled 2026-09-04:
      // the live site holds no track durations, so the minutes half of this
      // line must be absent rather than invented (it read "9 tracks · 54 min"
      // over every tracklist). trackFoot() omits it when the rows carry none.
      recTrackCount: this.trackFoot(this.recTrackRows(pair)),
      // ⚠ THE ONLY CONTROL THAT CAN SHORTEN A 41-TRACK LIST, and it was an empty
      // string in a pointer-styled span with no handler (2026-09-09, code audit
      // 2c). It says what it does and it does it — toggleTracks is the same
      // handler the tracklist's own "more" row uses, so the two cannot disagree.
      recShowAll: (this.recTrackRows(pair) || []).length > 8 ? (st.allTracks ? 'Show fewer' : 'Show all') : '',
      recShowAllStyle: (this.recTrackRows(pair) || []).length > 8
        ? { color: 'var(--gold)', cursor: 'pointer' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // THE DESCRIPTION IS FULLY EXPANDED BY DEFAULT — Tom, 2026-08-25: "show
      // the full description text by default". The frame's 132px window and its
      // "Read all · N words" affordance are both gone; the full text renders.
      // SOURCE, RULED 2026-09-04: REC_NOTES[title] ← cleanDescriptionHtml(
      // Album.descriptionHtml). It was a paragraph about Passing Fires typed
      // here, so all 489 records claimed the same two days at Palisade. A
      // record with no description shows no note — the block collapses.
      recNote: (this.data('REC_NOTES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)] || '',
      // No description → no block, not a 16px gap between the tracklist and
      // the formats row.
      recNoteStyle: (this.data('REC_NOTES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)]
        ? { marginTop: '16px', fontSize: '13px', lineHeight: 1.65, color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' }
        : (this.missing('REC_NOTES', 'record', (pair ? pair[1] : '')), { display: 'none' }),
      // Under the cover now, so it opens the block rather than closing the
      // right column: a short rule above it, and the chips sit directly under
      // the catalogue line they belong to (2026-09-06).
      recFormatsStyle: { marginTop: '12px', display: 'flex', flexWrap: 'wrap', gap: '8px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      // The same chips the ATH release page carries, so both album-level pages
      // name their formats the same way. A sold-out format keeps its chip rather
      // than vanishing, per the buy sheet's own rule.
      // SOURCE, RULED 2026-09-04: BUY rows [format, note, price, status] ←
      // Album.variants[] {title, availableForSale, quantityAvailable}. These
      // were the three literals LP / CS / DL, so a cassette-only release still
      // advertised vinyl. Prices stay in the buy sheet — the chips name formats
      // only, which is the standing rule.
      // ── RELEASE STATE IS A PILL, ONCE (2026-09-06, Tom) ──────────────────
      // ⚠ recKicker — the only reader of PAIRS[5] — was deleted with the
      // duplicated header, which left the release state surfaced NOWHERE: a
      // preorder record looked identical to a released one, and PAIRS[5] became
      // an orphaned table element. The complaint was that the kicker's value
      // was WRONG ("out now, which it isn't"), not that the fact was unwanted.
      //
      // So it is a pill in the block under the cover — where all the metadata
      // now lives — and it appears exactly once, which satisfies both halves of
      // the ask. Its own treatment (filled gold) rather than an outlined format
      // chip, because it is a STATE and not something you can choose.
      //
      // Only PREORDER is marked. "Out now" on a released record is the default
      // condition of every record on the site, and a pill saying so is the same
      // noise the removed kicker was: absence is the honest way to say normal.
      recFormatChips: (pair && pair[5] === 'preorder'
        ? [['Preorder', 2]] : []
        ).concat((this.data('BUY') || [])
        .map(b => [b[0], b[3] === 'sold-out' || b[3] === 'soldout' || b[3] === 0 ? 0 : 1])
        .filter((f, i, a) => f[0] && a.findIndex(x => x[0] === f[0]) === i))
        .map((f, i) => ({
        key: 'rf' + i,
        label: f[0],
        box: {
          display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '30px', padding: '0 11px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
          borderRadius: '4px',
          border: '1px solid ' + (f[1] === 2 ? 'var(--gold)' : f[1] ? 'rgba(138,106,18,0.5)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
          background: f[1] === 2 ? 'var(--gold)' : 'transparent',
          letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
          color: f[1] === 2 ? 'var(--ongold)' : f[1] ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink40)'
        }
      })),
      // The chips name the formats now, so the line beside them says the thing
      // they cannot — where the prices live.
      // "Prices in the buy sheet" DELETED 2026-09-04 at Tom's request — it was
      // a note about where prices live, addressed to nobody: a visitor reading
      // the chips does not need to be told the buy sheet exists, and it read as
      // leftover instruction. The chips name the formats; the sheet has the
      // prices; nothing in between needs saying.
      recBuyStyle: narrow
        ? { flex: 2, minWidth: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', gap: '6px', height: '42px', padding: '0 8px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', cursor: 'pointer', border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', background: 'rgba(219,191,61,0.06)', color: 'var(--gold)', fontWeight: 700, whiteSpace: 'nowrap' }
        : {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', height: '42px', padding: '0 16px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '7px', cursor: 'pointer', flexShrink: 0, whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', background: 'rgba(219,191,61,0.06)', color: 'var(--gold)', fontWeight: 700
      },
      isAbout: view === 'about',
      isAlbum: view === 'album' && !starHoverNarrow,
      releases: releases,
      artists: artistRows,
      tour: tour,
      tracks: tracks,
      // Counted, not typed (designer request 2) — the same reader the album
      // page's foot uses. No durations in the live data yet, so the "· M min"
      // half appears only once there are.
      trackMeta: pair ? (this.trackFoot(this.recTrackRows(pair)).replace(' tracks', '').replace(' track', '') || 'pending') : 'pending',
      albumCover: {
        width: '100%', aspectRatio: '1', borderRadius: '7px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)',
        background: pair && pair[3]
          ? 'url(' + this.res(pair[3]) + ') center/cover no-repeat, #0a0e1a'
          : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 8px, transparent 8px 16px)'
      },
      albumTitle: titles[0],
      albumArtist: titles[1],
      albumArtistStyle: { marginTop: '5px', fontSize: '13px', fontStyle: pair ? 'normal' : 'italic', color: 'var(--ink70)' },
      // FORMATS COME FROM BUY, NOT FROM THIS LINE (2026-09-04). It appended a
      // literal "LP / CS / DL" to every record's meta, so a cassette-only or
      // digital-only release advertised vinyl in the sidebar.
      albumMeta: pair
        ? (pair[2] + (() => {
            const f = (this.data('BUY') || []).map(b => b[0]).filter((v, i, a) => v && a.indexOf(v) === i);
            return f.length ? ('\u2002·\u2002' + f.join(' / ')) : '';
          })())
        : 'catalogue number and date pending',
      albumMenuOpen: !!st.albumMenu,
      toggleAlbumMenu: (e) => { e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ albumMenu: !st.albumMenu, trackMenu: null }); },
      albumDotsStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '40px', height: '40px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', fontSize: '14px',
        border: '1px solid ' + (st.albumMenu ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
        background: st.albumMenu ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : 'transparent',
        color: st.albumMenu ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)'
      },
      albumMenuRows: [
        this.menuRow('Add the record to the queue'), this.menuRow('Play next'), this.menuRow('Add the record to a playlist'),
        this.menuRow('Bandcamp', '↗'), this.menuRow('Spotify', '↗'), this.menuRow('Apple Music', '↗')
      ],
      trackMenuRows: [
        this.menuRow('Add to the queue'), this.menuRow('Play next'),
        this.menuRow('Find on Spotify', '↗')
      ],
      // THE COLUMN'S DESCRIPTION IS THE RECORD'S (2026-09-04). This was a
      // sentence describing what the column would one day print. Same source as
      // the album page: REC_NOTES[title] ← cleanDescriptionHtml(
      // Album.descriptionHtml), with a pasted tracklist stripped on the way in
      // by sanitize.looksLikeTracklist. No description → the block collapses.
      about: (this.data('REC_NOTES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)] || '',
      aboutStyle: {
        marginTop: '8px', fontSize: '12.5px', lineHeight: 1.65, fontStyle: 'italic',
        color: 'var(--ink55)', textWrap: 'pretty',
        display: '-webkit-box', WebkitBoxOrient: 'vertical',
        WebkitLineClamp: st.allAbout ? 'unset' : 5, overflow: 'hidden'
      },
      // A HEAD AND A LIVE TOGGLE OVER NOTHING (2026-09-04). Wiring `about` to
      // REC_NOTES left "ABOUT THIS RECORD / SHOW MORE" standing on a record
      // with no description. Head, body and toggle stand or fall together.
      aboutHeadStyle: (this.data('REC_NOTES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)]
        ? { marginTop: '22px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.18em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      aboutMoreStyle: (this.data('REC_NOTES') || {})[this.recKey(pair)]
        ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', marginTop: '2px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', cursor: 'pointer' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // ONE BIO SOURCE, ONE WORDING (2026-09-04). The column carried a third of
      // each — "Bio pending — their own Bandcamp copy." typed into the markup.
      // Same reader as the album and artist pages: ATH_BIOS[name] ←
      // Album.artistBio, no fallback, and the file's one pending sentence.
      colBio: (() => {
        const who = pair ? pair[0] : '';
        return this.artistBio(who) || ((this.props.site || (typeof window !== 'undefined' && window.__DC_SITE))
          ? ''
          : 'Biography pending — the artist page carries the full note, discography and press shots.');
      })(),
      colBioStyle: (() => {
        const has = !!this.artistBio(pair ? pair[0] : '');
        return {
          display: 'block', marginTop: '4px', fontSize: '12px', lineHeight: 1.6,
          fontStyle: has ? 'normal' : 'italic', color: has ? 'var(--ink70)' : 'var(--ink40)', textWrap: 'pretty'
        };
      })(),
      aboutMore: st.allAbout ? 'Show less' : 'Show more',
      toggleAbout: () => this.setState({ allAbout: !st.allAbout }),
      tracksMore: TR_ALL.length > 5 ? (st.allTracks ? 'Show less' : 'Show all ' + TR_ALL.length) : '',
      // Nothing to expand on a short tracklist: no dead control.
      moreTracksStyle: TR_ALL.length > 5
        ? { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', marginTop: '2px', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', cursor: 'pointer' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      toggleTracks: () => this.setState({ allTracks: !st.allTracks, trackMenu: null }),
      moreStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', marginTop: '2px',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px', letterSpacing: '0.16em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)', cursor: 'pointer'
      },
      portraitStyle: {
        width: '68px', height: '68px', flexShrink: 0, borderRadius: '5px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
        // The artist's portrait from INDEX, never a name check.
        background: (() => { const r = pair ? this.data('INDEX').find(x => x[0] === pair[0]) : null; return (r && r[1]) ? ('url(' + this.res(r[1]) + ') center/cover no-repeat') : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 6px, transparent 6px 12px)'; })()
      },

      browseOpen: !!st.browseOpen,
      // ── THE AUDIO BUTTON · THE CHROME'S OWN HOVER-AUDIO CONTROL (2026-09-07) ─
      // Declared beside Browse because it sits beside Browse. Tom's brief in
      // full: a button reading "Audio" between Browse and Grid|Map; mouse over
      // for the on/off; when it is ON the button shows the visualization
      // instead of the word. "It's a little more subtle."
      //
      // WHY THIS IS THE BETTER HOME, recorded because it retires two controls:
      // the meter and the switch both want to be somewhere permanent and out of
      // the way, and the chrome is the only surface every route already has.
      // The page-level copies each existed because the chrome had nothing — the
      // floater because a flat page has no transport, the drops card because the
      // ATH deck has neither. With the button here, both are a second control
      // for one setting, which is what the floater's own gate forbids.
      //
      // ON is the gold tint and the bars; OFF is the word alone with a
      // transparent border, the same ghost treatment Explore uses. The held
      // state is a TINT, never a slab — the 2026-09-01 ruling that governs
      // Grid|Map and both pill pairs.
      // ── HOVER INTENT, BECAUSE THIS BUTTON IS IN A PATH (2026-09-07, Tom) ────
      // "Now that the hover audio is there, when I hit Browse, I can't get from
      // the Browse button to the Browse menu without it disappearing."
      //
      // Correct, and it is this button's fault rather than Browse's. Audio sits
      // immediately right of Browse, so the cursor's path from the Browse button
      // down to its menu crosses this one — and panelEnter fires on the first
      // pixel, which closes every other panel by design (PANELS is exclusive).
      // A control that opens on contact cannot sit in a route between two parts
      // of another control.
      //
      // So opening WAITS for intent: 160ms of the cursor actually resting here,
      // cancelled the moment it leaves. Crossing takes a fraction of that, so
      // the Browse menu survives the trip; stopping on Audio still opens it with
      // no perceptible delay. Only the OPEN is delayed — leaving is unchanged,
      // and a CLICK is immediate, because a click is intent already stated.
      //
      // The timer is its own handle, not panelLeave's _closeT: they can be armed
      // at the same moment (leaving Browse while entering Audio) and sharing one
      // would have each cancelling the other.
      // The button is absent at narrow entirely (14c) — the same rule that
      // removes the transport's switch and the floater there. ⚠ THE REASON IS
      // ROOM, NOT CAPABILITY (revised 2026-09-10): a phone DOES have hover, by
      // touch, and sets the mode from the ☰ sheet's Audio row.
      //
      // ⚠ AND IT IS ABSENT IN STAR VIEW (2026-09-07, Tom: "it doesn't work in
      // Starmap mode, but for grid mode I think it makes sense to have it up
      // there. Keep it in the transport for Starmap."). The star field is the
      // one surface where the transport is always up — hover audio is armed by
      // default there, so something is always sounding and the bar is always
      // earned — and the transport's own switch sits inches away with its own
      // five bars beside it. A header copy there is the two-controls-eight-
      // pixels-apart case exactly. So: GRID AND THE FLAT PAGES get the header
      // button, STAR VIEW keeps the transport's, and the curator map keeps the
      // corner copy Tom ruled onto it. One control per surface, never two.
      // ── ONE HOME, EVERY SURFACE (2026-09-07, Tom) ───────────────────────
      // "The new audio header hover audio system needs to be installed across
      // the horizon also. So no more floater. It's in the header now."
      //
      // So the ath-map exclusion is gone with the corner switch it deferred to.
      // The header button carries all seven ATH landing panels, the curator map
      // included — which is the right shape and was the one I flagged for a
      // ruling: the map suppresses the transport entirely (Tom, 2026-09-03), so
      // before this the map NEEDED its own control and now it does not.
      //
      // What is left is one rule with one exception: THE HEADER OWNS IT
      // EVERYWHERE EXCEPT STAR VIEW, where the transport is always up and owns
      // it instead (Tom: "keep it in the transport for Starmap"). Two homes,
      // divided by whether a transport is present, and never both at once.
      // ⚠ V2 · IN THE HEADER IN STAR MODE TOO (2026-09-07, Tom: "it's to be in
      // the header also in star mode"). The isStar exclusion is gone with the
      // transport's own switch. There is now exactly ONE home for this control
      // on every surface at every width above 700 — no route exception, no panel
      // exception, no second copy anywhere — which is the first time that has
      // been true. The switcher stays absent below 700 (14c) because 390px of
      // header has no room for it — NOT because the mode is unavailable there:
      // that was true until 2026-09-10 and is not any more.
      //
      // ⚠ AND THE 09-10 REPLACEMENT IS ITSELF SUPERSEDED (2026-09-14). It read
      // "the ☰ sheet's Audio row is the phone's switcher, and
      // touchHoverBridge() makes hover real on a touch device" — both halves
      // false now: the sheet's whole modes block is hidden on a phone
      // (menuModesStyle) and the bridge is inert. Hiding this control is safe
      // for a simpler reason than either version gave: a phone has ONE audio
      // mode, so there is nothing to switch. The original sentence — "the mode
      // there is whatever the surface's default is, and the play controls
      // follow it" — was a bug written as a decision, and that warning stands.
      // ══ V2 · THE MODE CONTROLS ARE ALWAYS OPEN (2026-09-09, Tom) ═════════════
      // "Two lines: a header that takes one-third of the height, underneath it a
      // toggle… Drop down all visible. Somehow, it doesn't look crowded. It's
      // very clear what it is."
      //
      // A dropdown is for a LIST. These are two-state switches, and the panel
      // each one opened held exactly one toggle with exactly two sides — so
      // every interaction was a hover, a wait, and a click to reach a control
      // that could have been on screen the whole time. The panel was packaging
      // with nothing in it. Now both are permanently open, stacked inside the
      // row's own height: label on top, toggle under it.
      //
      // WHAT THIS RETIRED, all deleted rather than left defined: viewWrapStyle,
      // viewBtnStyle, viewBtnHoverStyle, viewPanelStyle, viewPanelOpen,
      // viewTitle, audioWrapStyle, audioBtnStyle, audioBtnHoverStyle,
      // audioWordStyle, audioPanelStyle, audioPanelOpen, the four hover-intent
      // handlers with their two timers, and both panel toggles. The 160ms hover
      // intent went with them — it existed because these panels sat in the
      // cursor's path between Browse and its menu and stole the trip. Nothing
      // opens on contact here any more, so there is no trip to steal.
      //
      // st.view2 and st.audio survive ONLY as PANELS keys for closeAllPanels;
      // nothing reads them now. Left in PANELS deliberately: removing a key from
      // that list is a separate change with its own blast radius.
      //
      // ONE LABEL PATTERN, TWO CONTROLS. Both stacks take modeStackStyle and
      // modeStackLabelStyle, so the pair cannot drift — the thing that made them
      // read as one pattern when they were buttons, kept now they are not.
      // ── THE WHOLE STACK IS ONE BROWSE BUTTON TALL (2026-09-09, Tom) ────────
      // "The whole height, including the header, needs to be the same height as
      // the browse button."
      //
      // So the two lines are not extra height, they are the SAME height,
      // subdivided: 9 label + 1 joint + 22 toggle = 32, which is Browse exactly
      // (8 + 1 + 19 = 28 snug). The first pass read the row's 46px as the budget
      // and made a 42px stack — taller than every control beside it, which is
      // what made it look like an inserted block rather than part of the run.
      //
      // The type goes down with it: 7px label, 8px sides. Small, and deliberately
      // so — a caption at 7px over an 8px toggle still reads because both are
      // uppercase mono with wide tracking, which is the most legible thing this
      // chrome has at small sizes. Below this the labels would have to go.
      //
      // CENTRED, not flush left (Tom, same message): the label belongs to the
      // toggle under it, and a caption offset from the thing it names reads as
      // pointing at whatever is left of it — which here is the previous control.
      // ── LABEL BESIDE THE TOGGLE, NOT ABOVE IT (2026-09-09, Tom) ───────────
      // "That looks bad. Make the toggle full height and move the label to the
      // left of the toggle."
      //
      // The two-line stack was the wrong trade and he called it: fitting a
      // caption AND a control into one button's height left the toggle at 22px
      // with 8px type — smaller than everything beside it, so the control that
      // matters most in the pair became the hardest to read. Side by side, the
      // toggle gets the row's full 32px (28 snug) and its own type back, and the
      // label costs width instead of height. Width is the cheaper resource here
      // — the snug tier recovered ~50px of it and Browse moving right freed the
      // run's left end.
      //
      // ONE WORD each: "View", "Audio". Beside a Grid|Map toggle, "View mode"
      // was saying the same thing twice — the toggle IS the mode, so the label
      // only has to name which one.
      modeStackStyle: narrow
        ? { display: 'none' }
        : { position: 'relative', flexShrink: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center',
            gap: snug ? '6px' : '7px' },
      // A caption for the control to its right, so it reads quieter than the
      // thing it names: 8px against the toggle's 9px, ink40 against ink55.
      //
      // ── AND THEY GIVE WAY TO THE DRAWER (2026-09-09) ───────────────────────
      // ⚠ A REGRESSION FROM ADDING THEM. The header's right-hand group is bound
      // by `gutter`, which on an open star drawer is drawerW + 16 — so at 1180
      // the run has to end at x784. With both labels in it, Explore ran to x865
      // and 65px of it sat UNDER the drawer, hit-tested by the drawer's own
      // heading: a control painted over and unclickable, clipped to a bare "E".
      // I measured the run in isolation (289px) and never against open === true
      // at the narrowest width, which is the one combination that could fail.
      //
      // THE LABELS ARE WHAT GOES, not the toggles: Grid|Map and Hover|Player
      // already say what they are, so the words are the redundant part of the
      // pair. Two labels plus their gaps is ~80px, which is the overflow almost
      // exactly. Only in this one case — snug AND star AND drawer open — because
      // that is the only place the run does not fit; at 1728 the same gutter
      // leaves 1272px of row.
      // ⚠ THE LABELS ARE BACK (2026-09-09). They were hidden at snug+star+open to
      // stop Explore running under the drawer; the wordmark now gives way there
      // instead and frees twice as much, so the labels no longer have to pay for
      // it. Tom's ruling settles the order: the name goes before any control's
      // label does, because the name is not a control.
      modeStackLabelStyle: !modeLabelsShown ? { display: 'none' } : {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px',
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        // ⚠ ink40 AT 8px IS 2.67:1 AND THAT IS NOT LEGIBLE. My own reasoning —
        // "a caption should read quieter than the thing it names" — is right
        // about hierarchy and I paid for it in contrast: 8px uppercase mono at
        // rgba(23,25,27,0.42) over white is below the 4.5:1 text minimum and
        // below even the 3:1 that only applies to headline-scale type, which
        // this is the opposite of. Tom asked for the label to be SMALLER; the
        // colour was mine.
        //
        // ink70 composites to ~6.8:1 here and still reads quieter than the 9px
        // gold selected side beside it, which is what the hierarchy actually
        // needed — smaller and darker, not smaller and fainter.
        // cInk70 rather than --ink70: over the landing photograph the caption
      // takes the header's light ladder like every other word in the run.
      color: cInk70, whiteSpace: 'nowrap'
      },
      // The divider between the two stacks (2026-09-08, Tom: "put the vertical
      // line between View Mode and Audio Mode"). It says these are two SEPARATE
      // settings that happen to be neighbours — one changes what you look at,
      // the other how you hear it. Taller now that it flanks two-line stacks,
      // and offset down so it centres on the toggles rather than on the labels.
      // modeRuleStyle DELETED (2026-09-09, Tom: "remove the vertical line between
      // them"). It was separating two controls that each already carry their own
      // name and their own bordered shell — so it was a third boundary in a run
      // that had two, and the labels do the separating better than a hairline
      // does. Not left defined: a style with no markup reads as intent.
      // ── THE TOGGLES · TWO-THIRDS OF THE ROW ────────────────────────────────
      // 28px against the label's 12 + 2 joint = 42, which is the snug row's full
      // height and 4px inside the wide one. So this costs no header height at
      // all, only width — which the snug tier had just recovered.
      //
      // Identical geometry for both, from one object, so View and Audio cannot
      // drift apart. The held side takes the gold TINT, never a slab
      // (2026-09-01), which is what Grid|Map has always done — this IS that
      // control, twice.
      // Both toggles read the SAME object, not two copies of it — the markup
      // needs two names, the design needs one geometry. modeSegStyle was a third
      // copy nothing referenced; deleted.
      // The shell plus the header's own hairline: on the landing it is drawn on
      // a photograph, where the theme's --hair20 is invisible (2026-09-09).
      // The phone's two, reading the SAME facts as the desktop pair above so
      // the two controls can never disagree about the mode.
      // ── THE SHEET'S MODE BLOCK (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────────────
      // Retired with it: phModeGroupStyle, phModeStackStyle, phModeCapLineStyle,
      // phViewBtnStyle/Label/Title/Click and phAudioBtnStyle/Label/Title/Click.
      // Narrow-only, because the desktop already carries these in its header.
      // ── AND THE AUDIO ROW GOES WITH THE VIEW ROW (2026-09-14) ─────────────
      // Consistency, by my own argument one key up: a switch with one reachable
      // side is not a switch. With hover off on a phone, Player is the only
      // audio mode — so the row would have offered a choice that does not
      // exist, which is exactly what greying out Map would have done.
      //
      // So the block goes entirely rather than becoming one row with one live
      // side, and the sheet opens on the nav list. That IS the phone product:
      // grid and player, no modes to pick.
      //
      // ⚠ menuViewRowStyle is now redundant on phone and is KEPT anyway — it
      // is the thing that documents which row went and why, and it is what a
      // desktop still reads. If the map ever returns to the phone, this key
      // and hoverArmed's narrow block are the two places to look.
      menuModesStyle: (!narrow || this.phoneNoMap()) ? { display: 'none' } : {
        display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '8px',
        padding: '13px 20px 15px', borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)'
      },
      // ── ONE COLUMN FOR THE CAPTIONS, ONE FOR THE TOGGLES (2026-09-10, Tom:
      // "left-aligned with the text and the same size, so they're not stepped
      // like that") ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // They were space-between with content-sized segments, so GRID|MAP and
      // HOVER|PLAYER came out different widths and their left edges did not
      // agree — the step he is pointing at. A fixed caption column and a fixed
      // segment width put both toggles on one left edge, and the sides inside
      // share that width equally rather than sizing to their own words.
      menuModeRowStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px' },
      // The View row only, so Audio is unaffected. display:none rather than
      // omitted markup, because the guide's "view" anchor still resolves
      // against it and guideAnchorRect() filters for a VISIBLE anchor — an
      // absent element and a hidden one read the same to it, and a hidden one
      // keeps the template honest about what used to be there.
      menuViewRowStyle: this.phoneNoMap() ? { display: 'none' } : { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px' },
      menuModeCapStyle: {
        width: '46px', flexShrink: 0,
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink55)'
      },
      // The desktop segment's own shell, so the two are the same control.
      // ── segWrapStyle / gridSeg / mapSeg · RELOCATED, NOT DELETED ───────────
      // (2026-09-10, engineering: GridMapControl.tsx:185 reads
      // phoneStyle('segWrapStyle'), so dropping the name is a type error.)
      //
      // The phone's Grid|Map control MOVED from the header into the ☰ sheet:
      // 390px of header carries a mark, a name, a hamburger and a cart, and a
      // labelled pair could only fit by stealing from the label's own name.
      // Same control, same geometry, new home — so these three keep exporting
      // it rather than disappearing.
      //
      // Canonical names if you would rather repoint: the shell renders the
      // wrapper as menuSegStyle and the sides from menuViewModes[i].side
      // (which is menuSide(on, dead)). segWrapStyle === menuSegStyle exactly.
      segWrapStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '2px', padding: '3px',
        width: '168px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
        borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)' },
      gridSeg: menuSide(!isStar, false),
      mapSeg: menuSide(!!isStar, false),
      // 168px, not max-content: equal width is what un-steps the two rows.
      menuSegStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '2px', padding: '3px',
        width: '168px', flexShrink: 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',
        borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)' },
      // ── THE SHEET BUILDS ITS OWN SIDES (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────
      // ⚠ NOT modeSide(). That is the HEADER's builder and its unheld ink is
      // chosen for the ground the header sits on — the landing photograph — so
      // borrowing it painted "Map" in 0.78 cream on a white sheet: 1.1:1, and
      // the VIEW segment read as though it had one option. The audio row only
      // escaped because its unheld side is the struck-through dead one, whose
      // colour is overridden anyway. Reuse was the fault, not the value, so

      //
      // 26px VISUAL, 40px TARGET, by the file's own technique (the 14c queue
      // rows): the side is 40px and gives 7px back top and bottom, so it
      // occupies 26px of the segment and catches 40px of finger. This control
      // is narrow-only — the phone is the ONLY device it exists on — so the
      // ≥40px rule is at its most binding here.
      menuViewModes: [['grid', 'Grid'], ['map', 'Map']].map((m) => ({
        key: 'mvm' + m[0], name: m[1],
        side: menuSide((m[0] === 'map') === !!isStar, false),
        click: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          this.guideModeChosen('view', m[0]);
          // ⚠ AND THE SHEET CLOSES (2026-09-10, Tom: "I just toggled in mobile
          // to grid, and transport didn't come up"). It HAD come up — behind the
          // sheet. View is the one control in this block that NAVIGATES, and a
          // full-height panel left open over the page it just changed hides
          // everything the change produced: the grid, and the transport that
          // rises with it. Audio deliberately does NOT close — it is a setting,
          // and the segment moving under your finger is its feedback — so the
          // two are not the same act and must not behave the same.
          //
          // Same shape as openBrowseFromMenu, which already closes for exactly
          // this reason. Set before the route moves, in one setState.
          this.setState({ menuOpen: false });
          if (m[0] === 'map') goStarFn(); else goArtistsFn();
        }
      })),
      // ⚠ BOTH SIDES ARE LIVE NOW (2026-09-10). Hover was struck through here
      // because hoverArmed() forced Player on a phone; touchHoverBridge() ended
      // that, so the strike, the dead flag and the explanatory note all go with
      // it. A control that works must not be dressed as one that does not.
      menuAudioModes: [['hover', 'Hover'], ['play', 'Player']].map((m) => {
        const live = this.audioMode(st) === m[0];
        const dead = false;
        return {
          key: 'mam' + m[0], name: m[1],
          side: menuSide(live && !dead, dead),
          click: dead ? undefined : (e) => {
            if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
            this.guideModeChosen('audio', m[0] === 'hover' ? 'hover' : 'player');
            this.setState(m[0] === 'hover'
              ? { hoverAudio: true, started: false, paused: false, npPlay: null, trackIdx: null, extPlay: null, dropPlay: null, mapSeat: null }

      // One line, only where something is actually withheld.
      // Says what the gesture IS, since a phone cannot discover it by moving a

      // Beside the switch it explains, not under both rows (2026-09-10, Tom:

      viewSegStyle: Object.assign({}, modeSeg, { border: '1px solid ' + cHair }),

          click: (e) => {

      // Audio + Browse as one group, carrying the auto margin that pushes the

      audioBrowseGroupStyle: narrow
        ? { display: 'contents' }
        // Tighter with the buttons (2026-09-08): this gap was sized for Browse

        //
        // ── AND THE GROUPS NEED TO SEPARATE (2026-09-11, Tom: "that whole
        // section is not spaced out evenly") ───────────────────────────────
        // The unevenness was not in any one gap — every gap here was equal,
        // which was the fault. A caption sits 7px from the segment it names,
        // and at a 9–12px outer gap the next control was barely further away
        // than that: 1.3×. So VIEW/GRID/MAP/AUDIO/HOVER/PLAYER/⟳ read as one

        // ⚠ AND THE RUN IS TWO CONTAINERS, WHICH IS WHY THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT

        //
        // One value now governs the whole run, and it is the HEADER's, because
        // that is the container this span cannot change. 15px against the 6px
        // inside a caption/segment pair is 2.5× — enough to group the pairs
        // without a rule, and identical on both sides of the seam.
        : { marginLeft: 'auto', display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', flexShrink: 0,

      // ── V2 · THE TWO AUDIO MODES ───────────────────────────────────────────
      // Hover / Player, one held. A toggle says these are THE two and one is

      //
      // The names are what the SITE is while set, not what the switch does:
      // hover mode removes every play button on every page, which is a bigger

      // A control that explains itself every time you look at it is teaching the

      //
      // RETIRED HERE, none left defined: audioBtnHoverStyle and audioWordStyle

      audioModes: [
        ['hover', 'Hover'],
        ['play', 'Player']

        const on = this.audioMode(st) === m[0];
        return {
          key: 'am' + m[0], name: m[1],
          side: this.modeSide(on, snug, modeLadder),
          // Setting the mode ends the lesson: the coach mark pointing here has
          // been answered, so it closes and does not return.
          click: (e) => {
            if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
              // ⚠ SWITCHING TO HOVER STOPS THE PLAYER (2026-09-09, Tom: "a song is
          // playing and I can't stop it… I'm not hovering on anything"). Hover
          // mode removes the transport AND every play control, so anything left
          // sounding from player mode had nothing on the page able to stop it.
          // The mode change is the stop: started, paused, npPlay, the queue mark
          // and any external episode all come down with it. Going the other way

          this.guideModeChosen('audio', m[0] === 'hover' ? 'hover' : 'player');
          this.setState(m[0] === 'hover'
            ? { hoverAudio: true, started: false, paused: false, npPlay: null, trackIdx: null, extPlay: null, dropPlay: null, mapSeat: null }
            : { hoverAudio: false });
          }
        };
      }),
      // ── "▶︎ Play the drop" NOW LIVES ON THE ROW (2026-09-15) ──────────────

      // Two lessons kept here, because both cost a drop:

      }),
      // ── THE PANEL HANGS OFF THE BUTTON (2026-09-09, Tom: "the browse menu is

      toggleBrowse: (e) => {

        if (e) { const at = this.anchorLeft(e, 396); if (at != null && at !== st.browseAt) this.setState({ browseAt: at }); }

      browseLeave: () => { if (narrow) return; this.panelLeave('browseOpen'); },

      // which drew a hard horizontal edge across the whole width — and the
      // label lockup overflows its own 72px header, so that edge landed
      // straight through the mark. Any fixed inset does: the lockup's height
      // is the brand's, not a number we choose.
      //
      // So the wash is the whole stage and the HEADER rises above it instead
      // (guideLift on headerStyle). The controls stay lit because the thing
      // they live in stays lit, as one piece, with no edge to place. The hero's
      // own lockup below the header does get washed, which is right: it is part
      // of the page being quietened, not part of the controls being explained.
      // ── THE SHADE IS A WAY OUT (2026-09-11, Tom: "if I click any of the
      // shaded area on this screen, the guide will go away… that just helps
      // people get out of it quicker") ────────────────────────────────────
      // ⚠ THIS WAS pointerEvents: 'none' ON PURPOSE and the purpose still
      // holds — §17, intent outranks teaching, the site stays usable while it
      // is being explained. What changed is which surface carries that.
      //
      // The HEADER is lifted above this scrim (guideLift), and the header is
      // where every control the card describes actually lives: Browse, View,
      // Audio, the theme, Explore. Those stay lit and directly clickable, so
      // the visitor can still act on the thing being explained mid-sentence.
      // Only the WASHED part — the page behind, which the card has just
      // dimmed and covered — becomes a dismiss target.
      //
      // And a dimmed backdrop under a card already promises this. Clicking
      // through it to a control you can barely see was the surprising
      // behaviour: you would hit a cover you could not read and get a record
      // page you did not ask for. Now the lit part acts and the shaded part
      // closes, which is what the two shades were already saying.
      guideScrimStyle: st.guideIntro
        ? { position: 'absolute', inset: 0, zIndex: 30, background: 'rgba(12,14,17,0.38)',
            cursor: 'default', opacity: st.guideStep > 0 || this.rm() ? 1 : 0,
            transition: this.rm() ? 'none' : 'opacity 320ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // Dismiss, not start: clicking away is declining the guide, so it takes
      // the same path as Skip and must not be counted as having begun it.
      guideScrimClick: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        this.guideIntroEnd(false);
      },
      // The two holes: absolutely placed over the header's own control run, cut
      // by painting the chrome's ground back over the scrim. Cheaper and
      // steadier than a box-shadow spread, and it cannot drift when the row
      // steps at snug.
      // ── UNDER THE CONTROLS IT DESCRIBES, NOT THE STAGE'S EDGE ─────────────
      // (2026-09-10, Tom: "in grid mode it's pretty much under the question
      // mark, but in star mode, when the sidebar is there, it's way over.")
      //
      // Both halves of that are one fault: right: 24px is the STAGE's gutter,
      // which happens to sit near the "?" on a grid page and lands squarely on
      // the drawer on the star route — the card was covering the very column
      // the visitor is being told about. It never pointed at anything; it
      // looked right once by coincidence.
      //
      // It reads the same anchor the context tips read, so all four Guide
      // surfaces are positioned by one rule: hang off the control you are
      // describing. The toggles sit left of the drawer, so the card clears it
      // by construction rather than by a second branch.
      guideIntroCardStyle: st.guideIntro
        ? { position: 'absolute',
            top: (st.guideAt ? Math.round(st.guideAt.bottom + 10) : 86) + 'px',
            right: (st.guideAt ? Math.max(24, Math.round(st.guideAt.right)) : 24) + 'px',
            zIndex: 32, width: '320px',
            display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '14px',
            padding: '18px 20px 16px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '8px',
            // ⚠ OPAQUE, NOT var(--chrome) (2026-09-10, Tom: "the mask shades the
            // pop-up"). --chrome is rgba(255,255,255,0.92) — 8% transparent,
            // which is right for a header sitting on the page's own ground and
            // wrong for a card sitting on a 38% dark wash: the wash read straight
            // through it and greyed the copy. The card was never BEHIND the scrim
            // (z-32 over z-30) — it was translucent OVER it, which looks identical
            // and is a different bug. The context tip takes the same treatment.
            border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', background: 'var(--bg)',
            boxShadow: '0 18px 48px rgba(0,0,0,0.22)', pointerEvents: 'auto',
            opacity: st.guideStep > 0 || this.rm() ? 1 : 0,
            transform: (st.guideStep > 0 || this.rm()) ? 'none' : 'translateY(-8px)',
            transition: this.rm() ? 'none' : 'opacity 260ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1), transform 260ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1)' }
        : { display: 'none' },
      // ── THE NAME OF THE SITE YOU ARE LOOKING AT ──────────────────────────
      // Dynamic across every Stellar Atlas label: Northern Spy, NNA Tapes,
      // Egghunt, Whited Sepulchre, Across the Horizon.
      //
      // ⚠ siteName, NOT SHORT_NAME. SHORT_NAME is the abbreviation map (NSPY,
      // NNA…), an object — printing it gave "[object Object]".
      //
      // ⚠ AND isAth ON TOP OF IT, which is rule 1 the other way round. siteName
      // keys on IDENTITY (athSite), so a Northern Spy visitor reading ATH's
      // pages was told "Experience Northern Spy your way" while looking at
      // Across the Horizon. This sentence is about the site on screen, and
      // that is a ROUTE question.
      guideIntroTitle: 'Experience ' + (isAth ? 'Across the Horizon' : (siteName || 'this label')) + ' your way',
      guideIntroTitleStyle: { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '17px',
        fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: '-0.01em', lineHeight: 1.2, textTransform: 'none', color: 'var(--ink)' },
      guideIntroLede: 'Choose how you see the catalogue and how you hear it.',
      guideIntroLedeStyle: { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '12.5px',
        lineHeight: 1.5, letterSpacing: 'normal', textTransform: 'none', color: 'var(--ink70)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      // Each block arrives with its control. Under reduced motion both are
      // already at step 3, so they simply exist.
      guideViewBlockStyle: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '3px',
        opacity: st.guideStep >= 1 ? 1 : 0, transition: this.rm() ? 'none' : 'opacity 240ms linear' },
      guideAudioBlockStyle: { display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '3px',
        opacity: st.guideStep >= 2 ? 1 : 0, transition: this.rm() ? 'none' : 'opacity 240ms linear' },
      guideKickerStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',

      guideLineStyle: { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '12.5px',

      guideStartStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', minHeight: '34px', padding: '0 14px',

      guideSkipStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', minHeight: '34px', padding: '0 4px',
        cursor: 'pointer', color: 'var(--ink55)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace",
        fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      // ── THE CONTEXT TIP · one card, two anchors ────────────────────────────
      // The old coach mark's visual language, generalised: same hairline, same
      // chrome ground, same mono kicker over an Archivo sentence. What changed
      // is that the copy comes from the registry rather than a ternary, so a
      // fifth tip is a data row and not another branch.
      guideTipOn: !!st.guideTip,
      guideTipCardStyle: (() => {
        const t = this.guideTip(st.guideTip);
        if (!t) return { display: 'none' };
        const base = {
          position: 'absolute', zIndex: 28, width: '300px',
          display: 'flex', flexDirection: 'column', gap: '7px',
          padding: '14px 16px 13px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '8px',
          border: '1px solid var(--goldsoft)', background: 'var(--bg)',
          boxShadow: '0 14px 38px rgba(0,0,0,0.18)', pointerEvents: 'auto',
          animation: this.rm() ? 'none' : 'sa-coach-in 220ms cubic-bezier(.4,0,.2,1) both'
        };
        // ⚠ UNDER THE CONTROL IT NAMES, measured at open (guideAnchorRect).
        // RIGHT-ALIGNED to the control rather than left, because these controls
        // sit in the right half of the run and a 300px card hanging from their

        // was right-anchored for in the first place. Clamped to the stage's own

        // THE MAP TIP HAS NO CONTROL TO POINT AT — it is about the field
        // itself, so it sits in the field's own bottom-left corner, on the

        // floating at some other inset.
        if (t.anchor === 'field') {
          return Object.assign(base, { left: '24px', bottom: (this.starTailPx(st) + 24) + 'px' });
        }
        const at = st.guideAt;
        // No anchor found is not a crash and not a guess: the card falls back
        // to the stage's own gutter, which is where it used to live for every
        // tip. Visible and inside the frame, just not pointing.
        if (!at) return Object.assign(base, { top: '86px', right: '24px' });
        return Object.assign(base, {
          top: (at.bottom + 10) + 'px',
          right: Math.max(24, Math.round(at.right)) + 'px'
        });
      })(),
      guideTipTitle: (this.guideTip(st.guideTip) || {}).title || '',
      guideTipTitleStyle: { fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.18em', textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--gold)' },
      guideTipBody: (this.guideTip(st.guideTip) || {}).body || '',
      guideTipBodyStyle: { fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '12.5px',
        lineHeight: 1.5, letterSpacing: 'normal', textTransform: 'none', fontWeight: 400,
        color: 'var(--ink)', textWrap: 'pretty' },
      guideTipActionsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '8px', marginTop: '1px' },
      guideTipCtaLabel: (this.guideTip(st.guideTip) || {}).ctaLabel || 'Got it',
      guideTipCtaStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', minHeight: '32px', padding: '0 11px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        border: '1px solid var(--hair20)', color: 'var(--ink70)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      // The hover tip's CTA is the only one that DOES something, and it does
      // exactly what it says — one press, in the toggle the visitor can undo.
      guideTipCta: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        const t = this.guideTip(this.state.guideTip);
        const id = this.state.guideTip;
        if (t && t.cta === 'player') { this.guideModeChosen('audio', 'player'); this.setState({ hoverAudio: false }); }
        this.emit('guide.action', { action: 'guide.action', tip: id });
        this.guideClose(id, true);
      },
      guideTipDismissStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', minHeight: '32px', padding: '0 4px',
        cursor: 'pointer', color: 'var(--ink40)', fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace",
        fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase' },
      guideTipDismiss: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.guideClose(this.state.guideTip, true); },

      // ── THE GUIDE CONTROL ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // A "?" beside the theme toggle. Deliberately the quietest control in the
      // run: help does not need prime header space (Tom, 09-10), it needs to be
      // findable when someone goes looking. It is NOT in EXPLORE — that word is
      // reserved for the Stellar Atlas universe and one menu meaning two things
      // is the ambiguity this vocabulary was locked to prevent.
      // ⚠ IT YIELDS TO THE DRAWER, NOT TO THE TIER (2026-09-10, corrected
      // twice). With the column open at the snug tier the "?" was the last 7px
      // the row did not have and Explore ran into it, so I hid it at snug
      // outright — which took it off the landing and every flat page too, where
      // there is no column and the run has the whole stage. Tom caught it:
      // "not seeing the question mark on the landing page's header."
      //
      // The tight case is SNUG PLUS A DRAWER, which is the only place the row
      // actually runs out. Help is still the right thing to move when it does —
      // it is the only item in the run that is not a control — and it does not
      // disappear: it takes the route the theme switch already takes, the header
      // where there is room and the menu sheet where there is not. One home per
      // width, never none.
      // ── A WORD, NOT A QUESTION MARK (2026-09-11, Tom: "wondering if there's
      // something better than a question mark that's not so silly-looking and
      // that means help") ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
      // THIS ROW IS MADE OF WORDS — Browse, View, Audio, Explore — and the ?
      // was the only glyph in it pretending to be one. A lone ? in a circle
      // reads as a quiz, and it was doing the job of a label while looking
      // like a state control: round, bordered, the exact shape of the theme
      // toggle beside it, which is a TOGGLE and has every right to be a glyph.
      // Two different kinds of thing wearing one shape.
      //
      // GUIDE, not HELP: it is what this file has always called the feature
      // (guideReplay, GUIDE_TIPS, guideArrive) and what the menu row already
      // says in words, so the header stops being the one place it is nameless.
      // It also happens to be the honest verb for a site built on a map.
      //
      // Shaped like EXPLORE rather than like the theme circle, because that is
      // what it is: a word you press that opens something.
      //
      // ⚠ cInk70, NOT cInk55 — I dimmed it to keep it "the quietest control in
      // the run" and took away its border in the same edit, which is two
      // reductions for a job that needed one. On the landing the header sits
      // over a 0.34→transparent scrim and Guide falls at 29% down it, so the
      // real ground there is bare photograph: at 0.6/9px it measured 1.80:1
      // over bright sky, against CLAUDE.md's 4.5:1 and this file's own ruling
      // that 2.67:1 at 8px is not legible. Losing the ring is ALREADY what
      // makes it quieter than Explore; the alpha was the part that made it
      // unreadable.
      guideBtnStyle: (narrow || (snug && isStar && open)) ? { display: 'none' } : {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: snug ? '28px' : '32px',
        padding: snug ? '0 8px' : '0 10px', boxSizing: 'border-box', flexShrink: 0,
        borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid transparent', cursor: 'pointer',
