        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: snug ? '9px' : '9.5px',
        letterSpacing: '0.16em', textTransform: 'uppercase'
      },
      guideBtnLabel: 'Guide',
      // The menu's own Guide row. The Explore row above it is phone-only —
      // on a wide window Explore is in the header — but Guide has to be here
      // at EVERY width, because the header's Guide button is the thing that
      // yields when the row runs out of room (it was a "?" until 2026-09-11).
      // Same shape as that row so the two read as a
      // pair of "about this place" entries rather than a destination and an
      // afterthought.
      menuGuideRowStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'space-between',
        minHeight: '46px', flexShrink: 0, borderTop: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9.5px', letterSpacing: '0.16em',
        textTransform: 'uppercase', color: 'var(--ink70)', cursor: 'pointer' },
      menuGuideClick: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        this.setState({ menuOpen: false });
        this.guideYield();
        this.guideIntroStart(true);
      },
      guideReplay: (e) => {
        if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
        this.guideYield();
        this.guideIntroStart(true);
      },
      guideStart: () => this.guideIntroEnd(true),
      guideSkip: () => this.guideIntroEnd(false),
      trPlayGlyph: this._sounding() ? '❙❙' : '▶︎',
      trPlayClick: () => this.togglePlay(),
      // ── SEEK · THE BINDING THE RAIL NEVER HAD (2026-09-11, engineering:
      // "the control is drawn to be pressed and exposes nothing to press…
      // the design has to expose the binding first") ──────────────────────
      // They are right, and right that both sides were waiting: npRailStyle
      // has carried cursor:pointer and a 40px target since the phone pass,
      // with no event attribute anywhere on the element.
      //
      // A FRACTION, not milliseconds. This file knows where the press landed
      // along the rail and nothing else — duration lives in REC_TRACKS, which
      // is the host's, and doing the arithmetic here would mean two owners of
      // one number and a design that silently lies whenever a duration is
      // absent. 0–1 is the whole of what the gesture actually said.
      //
      // Emitted through the existing action channel rather than a new
      // __DC_SEEK, so it needs no new plumbing: window.__DC_ON_ACTION with
      // action 'player.seek'. Their seek(ms) is then seek(fraction * duration)
      // on the side that owns duration.
      npSeek: (e) => {
        if (!e || !e.currentTarget) return;
        const r = e.currentTarget.getBoundingClientRect();
        if (!r.width) return;
        const x = (e.clientX == null ? 0 : e.clientX) - r.left;
        const f = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, x / r.width));
        this.emit('player.seek', { fraction: Math.round(f * 10000) / 10000, rec: npRec, track: st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx });
      },
      // ── PREVIOUS, ANSWERED (2026-09-09) ──────────────────────────────────
      // It was never deliberate: next was bound in this drop and previous was
      // not asked about in the same breath, so it fell between two answers. A
      // transport with one direction is a transport that lost an argument.
      //
      // Same shape as next: it steps within the record that is sounding, through
      // play(), and is ABSENT — not inert — at track 0 or with nothing playing.
      trPrevStyle: (() => {
        const t = this._playTuple(st);
        const has = t && t.rec != null && (t.track == null ? 0 : t.track) > 0;
        if (!has) return { display: 'none' };
        return narrow
          ? { width: '40px', height: '40px', flexShrink: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', fontSize: '12px', color: 'var(--ink)', cursor: 'pointer' }
          : { cursor: 'pointer' };
      })(),
      trPrevClick: () => {
        const t = this._playTuple(this.state);
        if (!t || t.rec == null) return;
        const prev = (t.track == null ? 0 : t.track) - 1;
        if (prev < 0) return;
        this.play(t.rec, prev);
      },
      // The album panel's gold ▶ PLAY and the record page's ▶ Play release both
      // drive play() for THEIR record, and read ❙❙ while it plays.
      albumPanelPlay: () => this.play(st.lock != null ? st.lock : undefined),
      // ⚠ THE LAST PLAY BUTTON IN HOVER MODE (2026-09-09, Tom, on the live
      // site: "I hover over a star and it shows the album — there's a play
      // button. There should be no play buttons in hover mode.") It was the one
      // control in this file whose style was written INLINE in the markup, so
      // playCtl could not reach it and the twenty-six gated siblings hid while
      // this one stayed. A style in the template is a style the mode cannot
      // gate; it lives here now, like every other play control.
      albumPanelPlayStyle: this.playCtl({
        flex: 1, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
        height: '40px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px',
        background: 'var(--gold)', color: 'var(--ongold)', fontWeight: 700, cursor: 'pointer'
      }),
      albumPanelPlayLabel: (st.started && !st.paused && st.npPlay === st.lock && st.lock != null) ? '❙❙ Pause' : '▶︎ Play',
      recPlayClick: () => this.play(recIdx != null ? recIdx : undefined),
      trPlayStyle: {
        width: (narrow ? '40px' : '30px'), height: (narrow ? '40px' : '30px'), flexShrink: 0,
        borderRadius: '50%', background: 'var(--ink)', color: 'var(--bg)',
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
        fontSize: (narrow ? '12px' : '9px'), cursor: 'pointer'
      },
      // ── NEXT TRACK IS BOUND (2026-09-09, code review §5) ──────────────────
      // It carried title="Next track", cursor:pointer and NO handler — the same
      // drawn-but-dead pattern as the strand ⋮ menus and the article read link,
      // and the third time this file has shipped one. It advances within the
      // record that is sounding, through play(), so the transport and every row
      // that watches npPlay/trackIdx agree about what is on.
      //
      // ABSENT rather than inert where there is no next: nothing playing, or the
      // last track. Same rule as every other play control here — a control that
      // cannot act is not drawn.
      trNextStyle: (() => {
        const t = this._playTuple(st);
        const rows = (t && t.rec != null) ? this.recTrackRows(this.data('PAIRS')[t.rec]) : [];
        const has = t && t.rec != null && rows.length > ((t.track == null ? 0 : t.track) + 1);
        if (!has) return { display: 'none' };
        return narrow
          ? { width: '40px', height: '40px', flexShrink: 0, display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', fontSize: '12px', color: 'var(--ink)', cursor: 'pointer' }
          : { cursor: 'pointer' };
      })(),
      trNextClick: () => {
        const t = this._playTuple(this.state);
        if (!t || t.rec == null) return;
        const rows = this.recTrackRows(this.data('PAIRS')[t.rec]);
        const next = (t.track == null ? 0 : t.track) + 1;
        if (next >= rows.length) return;
        this.play(t.rec, next);
      },
      transportStyle: {
        // ATH IS QUIETER: on Across the Horizon the player does not exist until
        // something is actually playing — no resting bar at the foot of a page
        // you are only reading. The label sites keep theirs, since the star map
        // and its hover audio are built around it.
        // Clandestine has no player: a services company's page does not play a
        // Northern Spy record. The group page has none for the same reason — it
        // is the parent company's landing page, with no catalogue of its own.
        display: ((isAth && !st.started) || clsPage != null || isGroup) ? 'none' : 'flex',
        position: 'absolute', left: (narrow ? '12px' : '24px'), bottom: (narrow ? '12px' : '20px'),
        // ── THE SCRUB GETS ITS OWN LINE ON A PHONE (2026-09-10) ──────────────
        // Restoring the rail was not enough: at 390 the bar's five FIXED groups
        // (now-playing 114 + controls 40 + meter 18 + queue 54, four 12px gaps,
        // 26px padding) consume 364 of 366px, so the one flexible child — the
        // scrub — sat pinned at its 64px floor, and the duration label inside
        // it left a 24px rail. A 24×4px seek target is unusable and there was
        // no elapsed readout at all, which is the opposite of "a player without
        // a position readout is not a player".
        //
        // Width was never available; HEIGHT was — the bar is 54px inside a 90px
        // reserve. So the phone bar WRAPS: the controls row stays as it is and
        // the scrub takes a full-width second line beneath it. 76px total,
        // still inside the reserve barShown already holds (104px), so no pane
        // moves. The desktop bar is one line and is untouched.
        zIndex: 8, height: (narrow ? '76px' : '58px'),
        flexWrap: narrow ? 'wrap' : 'nowrap',
        alignContent: narrow ? 'center' : 'stretch',
        // The desktop gutter is sized for the side panel; on a phone there is
        // no panel, so the player spans the screen instead of collapsing.
        right: narrow ? '12px' : gutter, borderRadius: '7px',
        // FLOATING, not a mask. On a phone the sheet runs underneath the player,
        // so the player is translucent and blurred and you can see the list
        // continue behind it instead of losing a row to an opaque bar.
        background: narrow
          ? (st.theme === 'light' ? 'rgba(255,255,255,0.82)' : 'rgba(16,18,21,0.78)')
          : 'var(--chrome)',
        backdropFilter: narrow ? 'blur(14px) saturate(1.4)' : 'none',
        WebkitBackdropFilter: narrow ? 'blur(14px) saturate(1.4)' : 'none',
        border: '1px solid var(--hair)',
        boxShadow: '0 10px 30px rgba(0,0,0,0.16), 0 2px 8px rgba(0,0,0,0.08)',
        alignItems: 'center', gap: '12px', padding: '0 12px 0 14px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
        overflow: 'hidden', transition: EASE
      },
      npBlockStyle: {        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px', minWidth: 0,
        // On a phone this is the only elastic child: everything else is a fixed
        // target, so the title/artist column absorbs whatever is left (14c).
        //
        // ⚠ BASIS 0, NOT auto — AND THAT IS A WRAPPING RULE, NOT A SIZE TWEAK
        // (2026-09-10). With flex-wrap on, the browser decides LINE BREAKS from
        // each child's flex BASE size and only shrinks within a line afterwards.
        // At basis auto this block measured 244px, so 244 + 82 of controls

        //
        // At basis 0 the line-break maths reads 0 + 82 + 54 = 136 ≤ 340, so the
        // record, the controls and the queue chip all hold line one and this
        // block then GROWS into the remainder. Only the scrub wraps, which is
        // what order:1 below is for. Two rows, 56px, inside the box.
        flex: narrow ? '1 1 0' : ('0 0 ' + (open ? 196 : 340) + 'px'),
        width: narrow ? 'auto' : ((open ? 196 : 340) + 'px'),
        transition: EASE
      },
      npCoverStyle: {

      },
      // ── THE TRANSPORT'S ⋮ IS A CONTROL, SO IT IS SIZED LIKE ONE ────────────

      },
      // ── TOP TRACKS PLAYS THEM (2026-09-09, code audit 2c) ─────────────────
      // Drawn as the artist page's primary play control with no handler. It is
      // the same intent the ⋮ menu already emits — 'queue.addArtist' — plus the
      // press: queue the artist's records and start the first one. playCtl still
      // gates the whole control, so it is absent in hover mode as before.
      artistTopTracks: () => {

        this.emit('queue.addArtist', { action: 'queue.addArtist', rec: null, track: null, format: null, source: 'artist-top-tracks' });

        padding: '11px 16px', minHeight: narrow ? '44px' : 0, boxSizing: 'border-box',

      // THE TRANSPORT NAMES WHAT IS PLAYING (2026-09-04, designer request 3).
      // It appended the literal "· track 01" to every record, so the bar said
      // track 01 whatever was playing. st.trackIdx is the row the play method
      // set; the title comes from the same REC_TRACKS reader the tracklist
      // uses, so the bar and the list can never name different tracks.
      npTitle: (() => {
        // ── THE BAR NAMES THE EPISODE WHEN AN EPISODE IS SOUNDING ─────────────
        // ⚠ MISSED BY THE PORT, AND IT MADE THE BAR LIE. playExt clears npPlay —
        // an episode has no PAIRS record — and npRec falls back to index 0 when
        // npPlay is null, so pressing an episode on ath-broadcast raised a
        // transport reading "Passing Fires · Jeremy Cunningham" over a playing
        // podcast, with ❙❙ showing so it read as authoritative. The one surface
        // whose whole job is to say what is playing was the one naming the wrong
        // thing.
        //
        // The exclusivity in play()/playExt is right and stays; what was missing
        // is this branch. The subject is read HERE, first, before npPair is
        // consulted at all — four display keys ask it in the same order (cover,
        // title, title style, artist), which is why each one leads with extPlay
        // rather than sharing a resolver: they answer different questions about
        // the same subject.
        if (st.extPlay) return st.extPlay.title || '';
        if (!barPair) return npStar ? (npStar.artist + ' · pending') : '';
        const rows = this.recTrackRows(barPair);
        const i = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
        const t = rows[i];
        // ── A TITLE TRACK IS NAMED ONCE (2026-09-10) ──────────────────────
        // The format is `Record · track`, which is right, but this catalogue
        // is full of title tracks — PAIRS[0][1] and REC_TRACKS row 0 are both
        // "Passing Fires" — and the bar printed "Passing Fires · Passing
        // Fires". It was invisible while the resting bar showed the stand-in;
        // cueing a record made it the FIRST thing the landing shows in Player
        // mode. When the track's name is the record's name there is one thing
        // to say, so say it once.
        return (t && t[0] && t[0] !== barPair[1]) ? (barPair[1] + ' · ' + t[0]) : barPair[1];
      })(),
      npTitleStyle: { display: 'block', fontSize: '12px', fontStyle: (barPair || st.extPlay) ? 'normal' : 'italic', color: npStar ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis' },
      // ⚠ THIS KEY IS THE TRANSPORT'S SECOND LINE, NOT A CLAIM ABOUT
      // AUTHORSHIP. Named npArtist because for a catalogue record that line IS
      // the artist — but what belongs there is whatever names the thing
      // sounding, and for an episode that is the strand: "Ambient Country" under
      // "Centripetal Force Records", not a record's artist from earlier.
      // A CUED RECORD IS NAMED, NOT DESCRIBED. This printed the italic
      // stand-in "artist · title from catalogue" whenever nothing was playing —
      // a label in a data slot, which rule 2 forbids and which is now
      // unnecessary: barPair carries the cued record, so the bar names a real
      // one. The stand-in survives only where there is genuinely nothing to
      // name (no catalogue at all), which is the one case it was written for.
      npArtist: st.extPlay ? (st.extPlay.artist || '') : (barPair ? barPair[0] : 'artist · title from catalogue'),

      // ── THE SCRUBBER IS BOUND, NOT DRAWN (2026-09-04, designer request 4) ─
      // Elapsed "0:41", width 11% and duration "6:12" were literal markup, so
      // the bar showed the same 11% of the same 6:12 for every track. All three
      // read NOW_PLAYING = [positionSec, durationSec], published by the host on
      // the transport callback. No numbers → 0:00 / no fill, which is what a
      // transport that has not started should show.
      // ⚠ THE BROKEN CLOCK, OUT FOR THE THIRD TIME (2026-09-09, code audit part
      // 3). `|| 0` on a position printed a confident 0:00, and '--:--' is the
      // placeholder glyph r53 already stripped from this same field. With no
      // NOW_PLAYING there is no time to state, so neither prints.
      npElapsed: (() => {
        const p = (this.data('NOW_PLAYING') || [])[0];
        return (typeof p === 'number' && isFinite(p)) ? this.mmss(Math.max(0, Math.round(p))) : '';
      })(),
      npDuration: (() => {
        const d = (this.data('NOW_PLAYING') || [])[1];
        if (d) return this.mmss(Math.round(d));
        // A CUED RECORD KNOWS ITS LENGTH. Nothing is playing, so there is no
        // POSITION to print and npElapsed stays empty — but the track's own
        // duration is a fact REC_TRACKS already holds, and printing it is what
        // makes the resting bar read as loaded rather than broken. Still null
        // where the track carries no duration: never 0:00, never --:--.
        return cueSecs ? this.mmss(cueSecs) : '';
      })(),
      npBarStyle: (() => {
        const np = this.data('NOW_PLAYING') || [];
        const pos = np[0] || 0, dur = np[1] || 0;
        const pct = dur > 0 ? Math.min(100, Math.max(0, (pos / dur) * 100)) : 0;
        return { display: 'block', width: pct.toFixed(2) + '%', height: '4px', borderRadius: '2px', background: 'var(--gold)' };
      })(),
      scrubStyle: {
        // ⚠ ON A PHONE TOO (2026-09-10, Tom). It was narrow-hidden, so a phone had
        // no elapsed time, no duration and no way to seek — and Player mode is
        // meant to feel like a player. Radio still has none: a live stream has no
        // position to show and nothing to seek to.
        display: (route === 'radio') ? 'none' : 'flex',
        // 100% on a phone forces the wrap and gives the rail the whole width;
        // the desktop's flex basis is unchanged.
        flex: narrow ? '1 1 100%' : '1 1 90px',
        minWidth: narrow ? 0 : '64px',
        maxWidth: narrow ? 'none' : (open ? 'none' : '340px'),
        marginTop: narrow ? '-2px' : 0,
        // ⚠ LAST IN THE FLEX ORDER ON A PHONE. The scrub sits third of six in

        order: narrow ? 1 : 0,

      },
      // ── THE RAIL IS 4px OF PAINT AND 40px OF TARGET ON A PHONE (2026-09-10)
      // It was hard-coded inline in the template at height:4px, so it could not

      // control, it is a decoration you occasionally hit. Same technique as the

      npRailStyle: narrow

            // backgroundColor, not background — see menuSide's note. With the

        // through negative margins, with backgroundClip keeping the PAINT at

        : { flex: 1, minWidth: 0, height: '16px', margin: '-6px 0', padding: '6px 0',
            boxSizing: 'border-box', backgroundClip: 'content-box', WebkitBackgroundClip: 'content-box',
            borderRadius: '2px', backgroundColor: 'var(--hair)', cursor: 'pointer' },
      // A live stream has no duration, so the scrubber is replaced outright and
      // the meter beside it does the work (7n's own note).

      // Hover audio — THIS CONTROL IS CORRECT AND SHIPPING. Do not remove it.
      //
      // RULING 6 (Tom, 2026-09-01): "The hover audio on and off switch is now

      // ⚠ AN EARLIER VERSION OF THIS COMMENT SAID "SUPERSEDED CONTROL … this

      // THE DEFAULT IS PER VIEW — ruling 7, not 2a. On any starmap view it is

      // ── RULING 7 · WHERE HOVER AUDIO IS ON BY DEFAULT ────────────────────
      // Tom, 2026-09-01. This REPLACES ruling 2a's flat "defaults OFF", which

      // ⚠ THE PER-VIEW SPLIT IS NOT DERIVED HERE ANY MORE (2026-09-07). It read:

      // Hover audio is armed by whose site it is and whether the surface is a

      // state, never a second thing as a side effect. Both pill pairs — the

      armHoverAudio: (e) => { if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation(); this.setState({ hoverAudio: true }); },

      //
      // So the switch is one control with two widths: OFF it is the word and the

        padding: (narrow ? '0 8px' : '0 6px 0 10px'), boxSizing: 'border-box', flexShrink: 0, whiteSpace: 'nowrap',
        borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer',
        border: '1px solid ' + (st.queue ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
        background: st.queue ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : 'transparent',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.1em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        color: st.queue ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)'
      }),
      // ⚠ A DEAD PHONE GATE REMOVED (2026-09-15 audit). This object opened with
      //   display: (vpWidth < 700) ? 'none' : undefined,
      //   display: 'flex', …
      // and the second `display` overwrote the first, so the gate never ran:
      // the count has always shown on a phone. Two reasons it goes rather than
      // getting promoted into a working gate:
      //   · CLAUDE.md specifies the control as "☰ Queue N" and says the queue
      //     must be reachable everywhere. N is the count; hiding it on the one
      //     device where the queue matters most contradicts that.
      //   · Its test read this.state.vp rather than the render's st — the r76
      //     §6 fault, which would have answered for the wrong viewport on a
      //     probe pass the moment it did start working.
      // Behaviour is unchanged by this edit. The line was never in effect.
      queueCountStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', minWidth: '20px',
        height: '20px', padding: '0 5px', boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '3px',
        background: st.qover ? 'var(--tile)' : 'rgba(219,191,61,0.16)',
        color: st.qover ? 'var(--ink55)' : 'var(--gold)', fontWeight: 500, letterSpacing: 0
      },
      // DERIVED FROM THE LIST, never a literal (§57). It read '13' : '12' —
      // DEFAULT_QUEUE.length baked in — so on the live site the chip said 12
      // while the panel beside it said "0 of 0 playable". Same defect as the
      // cart badge "2" (§55). qover is a TOOL-ONLY demo toggle ("show me the
      // thirteenth row"); see qList below.
      queueCount: String(qShown.length),
      qShuffleStyle: this.playCtl({
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: narrow ? '44px' : '32px', padding: '0 11px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '5px', cursor: 'pointer',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '9px',
        letterSpacing: '0.12em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        border: '1px solid ' + (st.qshuffle ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
        background: st.qshuffle ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : 'transparent',
        color: st.qshuffle ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)'
      }),
      toggleQShuffle: () => this.setState({ qshuffle: !st.qshuffle }),
      qOverStyle: {
        display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', height: '26px', padding: '0 9px',
        boxSizing: 'border-box', borderRadius: '4px', cursor: 'pointer',
        fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '8px',
        letterSpacing: '0.14em', textTransform: 'uppercase',
        border: '1px solid ' + (st.qover ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.6)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
        background: st.qover ? 'var(--tile)' : 'transparent',
        color: st.qover ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink55)'
      },
      toggleQOver: () => this.setState({ qover: !st.qover }),
      qOverLabel: ['Zero','One','Two','Three','Four','Five','Six','Seven','Eight','Nine','Ten','Eleven','Twelve','Thirteen','Fourteen','Fifteen'][qShown.length] || String(qShown.length),
      qRows: qShown.map((q, i) => {
        // ⚠ WHICH ROW IS PLAYING IS A DERIVED FACT (2026-09-09). st.qplaying was
        // a literal 1 in the initial state and nothing ever moved it, so the
        // queue lit its second row whatever the transport was doing. The row
        // that is on is the one whose record and track the transport names;
        // st.qplaying is the fallback for a host-published queue whose rows
        // carry no record.
        const qOnIdx = (() => {
          if (st.npPlay == null) return st.qplaying;
          const want = st.trackIdx == null ? 0 : st.trackIdx;
          const hit = qShown.findIndex(r => r[4] === st.npPlay && r[5] === want);
          return hit > -1 ? hit : st.qplaying;
        })();
        const on = qOnIdx === i, hov = st.qhover === i, dead = !q[3];
        // CUED, NOT PLAYED (2026-09-10). Set by cueSet only. A cued row is a
        // real, pressable record that this session has not played — so it
        // reads one step quieter than a history row and says so, and the
        // moment it is pressed queuePlayed writes a genuine row and the whole
        // cue drops away.
        const cued = q[6] === true;
        return {
          key: 'q' + i,
          box: {
            position: 'relative', display: 'grid',
            gridTemplateColumns: '18px 34px 1fr auto 22px', alignItems: 'center', gap: '10px',
            padding: '7px 22px', minHeight: '54px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            cursor: dead ? 'default' : 'pointer', overflow: 'hidden',
            borderBottom: '1px solid var(--hair)',
            background: on ? 'rgba(219,191,61,0.1)' : hov ? 'var(--tile)' : 'transparent',
            boxShadow: on ? 'inset 3px 0 0 #DBBF3D' : hov ? 'inset 3px 0 0 rgba(219,191,61,0.45)' : 'none',
            fontFamily: "'JetBrains Mono', monospace", fontSize: '12px'
          },
          handleStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', height: '40px', margin: '-8px 0', fontSize: '11px', color: hov ? 'var(--ink70)' : 'var(--ink40)' },
          // ⚠ THE SLEEVE, NOT AN UNCONDITIONAL HATCH (2026-09-11, engineering).
        // This drew the 135° "no artwork" mark for EVERY row — the only
        // unbacked hatch among the file's 52 res()-backed image draws — so a
        // record showed its cover in the transport and a hatch in the queue at
        // the same moment, which is what Tom noticed. Every other property on
        // this object was already conditional; the background alone was not,
        // which is the signature of a row never wired rather than a decision.
        //
        // q[4] is the PAIRS index this file already resolves on this exact
        // tuple shape (qOnIdx, above), and PAIRS[rec][3] is the front cover.
        // The hatch stays as the fallback it was always meant to be.
        cover: (() => {
          const p = (this.data('PAIRS') || [])[q[4]];
          return { width: '34px', height: '34px', borderRadius: '4px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
            border: '1px solid ' + (on ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--hair20)'),
            background: (p && p[3])
              ? 'url(' + this.res(p[3]) + ') center/cover no-repeat'
              : 'repeating-linear-gradient(135deg, var(--tile) 0 5px, transparent 5px 10px)',
            opacity: dead ? 0.4 : 1 };
        })(),
          titleStyle: { display: 'block', fontFamily: 'Archivo, Helvetica, sans-serif', fontSize: '13px',
            color: dead ? 'var(--ink40)' : on ? 'var(--gold)' : cued ? 'var(--ink70)' : 'var(--ink)',
            textDecoration: dead ? 'line-through' : 'none',
            overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
          artistStyle: { display: 'block', marginTop: '2px', fontSize: '9px', letterSpacing: '0.08em', color: 'var(--ink55)', overflow: 'hidden', textOverflow: 'ellipsis', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
          title: q[0],
          artist: dead ? q[1] + ' · unavailable' : cued ? q[1] + ' · cued' : q[1],
          dur: dead ? '—' : this.mmss(q[2]),
          durStyle: { fontSize: '9px', color: 'var(--ink40)', whiteSpace: 'nowrap' },
      // ⚠ A HOVER-PREVIEW BLOCK WAS MISTAKENLY ADDED HERE AND IS REMOVED
      // (2026-09-10). It belonged to the star column's tracklist and landed in
      // the QUEUE's row builder — same shape of object, different data (q[0],
      // dead, dur), so it read recIdx and t[0] that do not exist in this scope
      // and a colHovOn that was never declared. renderVals threw on every
      // route until this came out.
      //
      // And it should not be here on the merits either: a queue row is a list
      // entry you press, not a surface you sweep. Its own enter/leave below
      // light the row and nothing else.
          // Height was already collapsed this way; the width needed the same, and
        // --ink40 at rest was the same 2.67:1 in light.
        dotsStyle: { display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center', width: '40px', height: '40px', margin: '-8px -9px', borderRadius: '4px', fontSize: '15px', cursor: 'pointer', color: hov ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)' },
          ctx: this.openCtx('queueRow', null),
          enter: () => this.hoverEnter({ qhover: i }),
          leave: () => this.setState({ qhover: null }),
          click: () => { if (!dead) this.setState({ qplaying: i }); }
        };
      }),
      qSummary: (() => {
        const a = qShown;
        const p = a.filter(q => q[3]).length;
        return p + ' of ' + a.length + ' playable · ' + this.mmss(a.reduce((s2, q) => s2 + q[2], 0));
      })(),
      qOverCap: !!st.qover,
      // Reworded with the constellation button's removal (2026-09-10): the cap
      // is the QUEUE's, and the sentence explained a control that no longer
      // exists. An out-of-date comment is a bug (rule 7) and so is an
      // out-of-date sentence on screen.
      qCapNote: 'This queue holds ' + this.CAP + ' tracks. Remove one to add another.',
      // ⚠ qMakeStyle / qMakeClick DELETED 2026-09-10 with the constellation
      // button they drove. Rule 6: a key no template hole reads is not a fact,
      // it is a place for one to rot — and these two would have gone on
      // resolving cleanly in renderVals with nothing on screen to show it.
      // 'constellation.add' keeps its other caller, the ⋮ menu.
      // ── SHARE IS THE QUEUE'S ONE ACTION NOW (2026-09-10, Tom) ────────────
      // "Get rid of the Make a constellation button — I'm abandoning that for
      // now — and instead just have a share button that opens Apple, Spotify,
      // YouTube and a direct link."
      //
      // So the foot is Clear, then one row of four destinations. What went is
      // the gold constellation button: it was the queue's primary action and it
      // led to a feature that is being put down, which made the most prominent
      // control in the panel the least wanted one.
      //
      // ⚠ FOUR TARGETS, ONE ROW, NO PRIMARY. A link, and three services. None
      // of them outranks the others — a copied link is as much the answer as a
      // Spotify playlist — so none of them is a filled button. The old row
      // already read as text links rather than boxes and that was right; this
      // is the same row with the link folded in and the dead 'Apple' strike
      // removed.
      //
      // ⚠ AND EACH ONE EMITS RATHER THAN CONSTRUCTS. A queue is a list of
      // tracks; turning it into a Spotify playlist needs an authenticated call
      // this design cannot make, and guessing a URL would be a derived address
      // (r49). Every target emits 'queue.share' with its own name and the host
      // does the work. The direct link is the one we can answer here, and it
      // goes through the same confirmCopy the ⋮ menu uses.
      qShareRow: [
        ['Link', 'link', 'Copy a link to this queue'],
        ['Apple', 'apple', 'Open this queue in Apple Music'],
        ['Spotify', 'spotify', 'Open this queue in Spotify'],
        ['YouTube', 'youtube', 'Open this queue in YouTube']
      ].map((d, i) => ({
        key: 'qs' + i,
        label: st.copied === ('queue-' + d[1]) ? '✓ Copied' : d[0],
        title: d[2],
        style: {
          display: 'flex', alignItems: 'center', justifyContent: 'center',
          minHeight: '36px', padding: '0 12px', boxSizing: 'border-box',
          borderRadius: '5px', border: '1px solid var(--hair20)',
          color: st.copied === ('queue-' + d[1]) ? 'var(--gold)' : 'var(--ink70)',
          cursor: 'pointer', whiteSpace: 'nowrap', flex: 1
        },
        click: (e) => {
          if (e && e.stopPropagation) e.stopPropagation();
          this.emit('queue.share', { action: 'queue.share', target: d[1] });
          if (d[1] === 'link') this.confirmCopy('queue-link');
        }
      }))

    };
    // On a phone the curator map's own values override the star view's
    // same-named keys — applied after, so no key order in the literal matters.
    if (narrow && route === 'ath-map') Object.assign(__vals, this.phoneMap(st, W, H));
    return __vals;
  }
}
